## Android Project Exploration Report: HackersPub

Based on a thorough exploration of the codebase, here's a complete overview of the project:

### 1. **Overall Project Structure**

**Path:** `/Users/kodingwarrior/fediverse/hackerspub-android`

**Key Directories:**
- `/app/src/main/java/pub/hackers/android/` - Main Kotlin source code
  - `/ui/screens/` - Screen/page implementations
  - `/ui/components/` - Reusable UI components
  - `/ui/theme/` - Theme and styling system
  - `/data/` - Data layer (repository, local storage)
  - `/domain/` - Domain models
  - `/di/` - Dependency injection setup

### 2. **UI Framework**

**Framework: Jetpack Compose (100% Compose-based)**

**Key Dependencies:**
- `androidx.compose.ui` - Core Compose framework
- `androidx.compose.material3` - Material Design 3 components
- `androidx.compose.material.icons` - Material icons
- `androidx.navigation.compose` - Navigation between screens
- `androidx.datastore.preferences` - Local preferences storage
- `coil.compose` - Image loading and caching
- `hilt.android` - Dependency injection
- `apollo.runtime` - GraphQL client for API calls

**Material Design Version:** Material Design 3 (latest)

### 3. **All Screens & Navigation**

The app uses Jetpack Navigation with a bottom navigation bar. **8 main screens:**

| Screen | Route | Auth Required | File |
|--------|-------|---------------|------|
| Timeline | `timeline` | Yes | `/ui/screens/timeline/TimelineScreen.kt` |
| Notifications | `notifications` | Yes | `/ui/screens/notifications/NotificationsScreen.kt` |
| Explore | `explore` | No | `/ui/screens/explore/ExploreScreen.kt` |
| Search | `search` | No | `/ui/screens/search/SearchScreen.kt` |
| Settings | `settings` | No | `/ui/screens/settings/SettingsScreen.kt` |
| Sign In (Detail) | `signin` | - | `/ui/screens/auth/SignInScreen.kt` |
| Compose (Detail) | `compose` | Yes | `/ui/screens/compose/ComposeScreen.kt` |
| Post Detail (Detail) | `post/{postId}` | - | `/ui/screens/postdetail/PostDetailScreen.kt` |
| Profile (Detail) | `profile/{handle}` | - | `/ui/screens/profile/ProfileScreen.kt` |

**Navigation Features:**
- Bottom navigation bar (visible only on main screens)
- Conditional bottom bar based on logged-in state
- Deep link support for sign-in verification (`hackerspub://verify?token=&code=`)
- Dynamic screen visibility based on authentication

### 4. **Theming & Styling System**

**Theme Files:**
- `/ui/theme/Theme.kt` - Color schemes and MaterialTheme setup
- `/ui/theme/Spacing.kt` - Centralized spacing constants

**Color Schemes:**

**Light Theme:**
- Primary: `#262626` (dark gray/black)
- Surface/Background: `#FFFFFF` (white)
- Secondary: `#F5F5F5` (light gray)
- Error: `#DC2626` (red)
- Tertiary (muted text): `#737373`

**Dark Theme:**
- Primary: `#FAFAFA` (off-white)
- Surface/Background: `#262626` (dark gray)
- Secondary: `#404040` (medium gray)
- Error: `#EF4444` (lighter red)
- Tertiary (muted text): `#A3A3A3`

**Spacing System (4px base unit):**
```kotlin
xs = 4.dp
sm = 8.dp
md = 12.dp
lg = 16.dp
xl = 24.dp
xxl = 32.dp
```

**Border Radius System:**
```kotlin
sm = 4.dp
md = 6.dp
lg = 8.dp
xl = 12.dp
```

**Theme Features:**
- System dark/light theme detection (`isSystemInDarkTheme()`)
- Status bar color synchronization with theme
- CompositionLocal provider for font scaling (font size customizable 75-200%)

### 5. **Custom Components & Design Patterns**

**Core Reusable Components:**

1. **CompactTopBar** (`/ui/components/CompactTopBar.kt`)
   - Compact 48dp height top app bar
   - Supports navigation icon, title, and trailing actions
   - Used on all screens consistently

2. **PostCard** (`/ui/components/PostCard.kt`)
   - Main card displaying posts (messages/notes)
   - Features:
     - Author info (avatar, name, handle, timestamp)
     - Post content with HTML rendering
     - Media grid (1-4 images)
     - Quoted post preview
     - Reaction groups (emoji-based)
     - Engagement bar (replies, shares, reactions, quotes)
     - Repost indicator when shared

3. **HtmlContent** (`/ui/components/HtmlContent.kt`)
   - Custom HTML-to-Compose text renderer
   - Features:
     - Link detection and styling
     - Mention detection (clickable handles)
     - Hashtag support
     - HTML tag parsing (p, br, strong, em, del, code, pre, blockquote, lists, etc.)
     - Invisible span handling
     - HTML entity decoding
     - Font scaling support

4. **MediaGrid** (in PostCard.kt)
   - Responsive media layout
   - 1 image: full-width
   - 2 images: side-by-side
   - 3+ images: grid layout

5. **QuotedPostPreview** (in PostCard.kt)
   - Embedded quoted post display
   - Rounded surface with border
   - Shows author, avatar, and post excerpt

6. **EngagementBar** (in PostCard.kt)
   - Interactive buttons for: reply, share, react, quote, external share
   - Shows counts with abbreviated format (K, M)
   - Active state indicators (filled icons when user interacted)

7. **MentionAutocomplete** (`/ui/components/MentionAutocomplete.kt`)
   - Real-time mention suggestions as user types
   - Popup positioned at cursor location
   - Shows actor avatars and names

8. **ReactionPicker** (`/ui/components/ReactionPicker.kt`)
   - Bottom sheet for selecting emoji reactions
   - Shows available reactions with counts

9. **ErrorMessage** (component)
   - Full-screen error state with optional icon
   - Retry button support

10. **LoadingIndicator** (component)
    - Full-screen loading spinner
    - Used during initial data load

**Additional Utility Components:**
- `FullScreenLoading()` - Loading state
- `LoadingItem()` - Pagination loading indicator
- `HtmlContent()` - Advanced HTML text rendering
- `InAppBrowserUriHandler()` - In-app browser context provider

### 6. **Layout & Visual Design Approach**

**Key Patterns:**

- **Card-based Design**: Posts displayed in cards with dividers
- **Vertical Scrolling**: LazyColumn for efficient list rendering
- **Pull-to-Refresh**: Modern swipe-to-refresh pattern (Material 3)
- **Bottom Sheet Dialogs**: Used for reactions, shares, quotes
- **Floating Action Buttons**: Compose buttons on main screens
- **Tab Navigation**: Explore screen uses tabs (Local/Global)
- **List Items**: Settings and search results use ListItem composables

**Responsive Layouts:**
- Safe area insets (edge-to-edge with WindowInsets)
- Adaptive spacing based on screen size
- Font scaling (75-200% user preference)

**Engagement UI:**
- Inline emoji reactions with count badges
- Share/like indicators with visual feedback
- Reply threading with quote contexts
- Notification items with action icons

### 7. **Architecture Overview**

**MVVM Pattern with Compose:**
- Each screen has a corresponding ViewModel
- State management via Flow/StateFlow
- UI state collected with `collectAsState()`

**Data Layer:**
- GraphQL API via Apollo client
- Local SQLite cache (Apollo normalized cache)
- DataStore for preferences
- Session management via SessionManager

**Dependency Injection:**
- Hilt for DI
- AppModule configures Apollo, DataStore, and repositories

**Navigation:**
- Sealed classes for type-safe route definition
- NavController with Compose NavHost
- Deep link support with intent data parsing

**All Screen Files:**
1. `/ui/screens/timeline/TimelineScreen.kt`
2. `/ui/screens/timeline/TimelineViewModel.kt`
3. `/ui/screens/notifications/NotificationsScreen.kt`

**Core Component Files:**

### 9. **Key Design Features**

- **Minimalist Aesthetic**: Clean, text-first design inspired by Twitter
- **Monochromatic Base Colors**: Grays and blacks with accent colors for actions
- **Generous Spacing**: 4px base unit for consistent, breathing layouts
- **Typography-Focused**: Content-heavy with readable default sizes
- **Dark Mode Ready**: Full light/dark theme support with system preference
- **Accessible**: Large tap targets (48dp minimum for buttons), high contrast
- **Performance-Optimized**: LazyColumn with efficient item recomposition, image caching via Coil
- **Web-Aligned Design**: Consistent with hackers.pub web design system

This is a modern, well-structured Compose-based Android application following Material Design 3 principles with a focus on content delivery and user engagement features typical of a Fediverse social network client.

### 2. UI FRAMEWORK

**SwiftUI** - Modern, declarative framework exclusively
- Uses modern iOS 17+ APIs (@Observable, Environment, @State, etc.)
- Native SwiftUI components (TabView, NavigationStack, List, ScrollView)
- GraphQL client integration (Apollo)
- Kingfisher for image loading

### 3. THEMING & STYLING APPROACH

**Color Scheme:**
- Uses system colors: `.primary`, `.secondary`, `.tertiary` for light/dark mode compatibility
- Uses `light-dark()` CSS function in HTML rendering for automatic theme detection
- Accent color defined in `AccentColor.colorset`
- Color opacity patterns: `.opacity(0.1)`, `.opacity(0.2)`, `.opacity(0.15)` for subtle backgrounds
- Tags and badges: Color-coded (blue for followers, red for blocks, green for engagement states)

**Typography:**
- `FontSettingsManager` for customizable fonts across app
- Font size multiplier: 0.75x to 3.0x range
- System dynamic type support with override option
- Custom font families supported (Arial, Helvetica, Times New Roman, Courier, Avenir, etc.)
- Font availability detection

**CSS/HTML Styling:**
- Dynamic CSS generation based on user font preferences
- Line height: 1.35
- Block margins/padding carefully tuned: `0.25em` for paragraphs, `0.5em` for headings
- Code blocks: `border-radius: 3px` (inline), `6px` (pre)
- Blockquotes: Left border `3px` solid with secondary color
- Links: System blue `#007AFF`
- Headers scale: h1=1.5em, h2=1.3em, h3=1.1em

**Spacing & Layout:**
- Consistent padding: 16px (standard), 8px (compact), 4px (fine)
- Component spacing in VStack/HStack: 8px to 16px
- Safe area consideration with `ignoresSafeArea()`

### 4. SCREENS/VIEWS & DESIGN PATTERNS

### 6. VISUAL APPEAL - DESIGN DECISIONS

**What Makes the App Visually Appealing:**

1. **Hierarchical Typography:**
   - Clear distinction between headlines, bodies, and captions
   - Font weights (bold for names, semibold for subtitles)
   - Size hierarchy creates visual structure

2. **Consistent Spacing:**
   - 8pt/16pt rhythm throughout
   - Clear padding around cards and content
   - Dividers between list items for breathing room

3. **Color Usage:**
   - Minimal but strategic color accents (blue links, red for actions, green for shares)
   - Secondary colors for metadata (handles, timestamps)
   - Semantic coloring (red for blocks, blue for follows)

4. **Rounded Corners:**
   - Profile cards: 12pt
   - Quoted posts: 8pt
   - Button backgrounds: 8pt
   - Tags/badges: Full capsule (rounded to max)
   - Images: Full circle for avatars, maintains aspect ratio for content

5. **Visual States:**
   - Filled vs. outline icons (heart filled when reacted)
   - Color tinting (green for shared, red for reacted)
   - Opacity changes for disabled states
   - Loading states with ProgressView spinners

6. **Rich Content Rendering:**
   - Dual rendering modes (heavy WebView for articles, lightweight text for timelines)
   - Skeleton placeholders while loading
   - Smooth transitions and opacity fades
   - Proper aspect ratio handling for images in carousels

7. **Interactive Feedback:**
   - Long-press gestures for alternate actions
   - Context menus with sneak peek previews (560pt height)
   - Haptic feedback implied through state changes
   - Disabled states prevent interaction errors

8. **Motion & Transitions:**
   - `.transition(.opacity)` for smooth fades
   - `.combined(with: .scale(scale: 0.98))` for zooming transitions
   - Redacted placeholder with `.shimmering()` effect during loading
   - Tab switching with native NavigationStack transitions

9. **Dark Mode Support:**
   - `light-dark()` CSS for automatic theme handling
   - System color usage for automatic contrast
   - Opacity-based overlays work in both modes

10. **Accessibility:**
    - Semantic views with `.accessibilityAddTraits(.isButton)`
    - Accessibility labels on all interactive elements
    - Content descriptions for images via `alt` attributes
    - Proper text hierarchy for screen readers

     1→# Visual Companion Guide
     2→
     3→Browser-based visual brainstorming companion for showing mockups, diagrams, and options.
     4→
     5→## When to Use
     6→
     7→Decide per-question, not per-session. The test: **would the user understand this better by seeing it than reading it?**
     8→
     9→**Use the browser** when the content itself is visual:
    10→
    11→- **UI mockups** — wireframes, layouts, navigation structures, component designs
    12→- **Architecture diagrams** — system components, data flow, relationship maps
    13→- **Side-by-side visual comparisons** — comparing two layouts, two color schemes, two design directions
    14→- **Design polish** — when the question is about look and feel, spacing, visual hierarchy
    15→- **Spatial relationships** — state machines, flowcharts, entity relationships rendered as diagrams
    16→
    17→**Use the terminal** when the content is text or tabular:
    18→
    19→- **Requirements and scope questions** — "what does X mean?", "which features are in scope?"
    20→- **Conceptual A/B/C choices** — picking between approaches described in words
    21→- **Tradeoff lists** — pros/cons, comparison tables
    22→- **Technical decisions** — API design, data modeling, architectural approach selection
    23→- **Clarifying questions** — anything where the answer is words, not a visual preference
    24→
    25→A question *about* a UI topic is not automatically a visual question. "What kind of wizard do you want?" is conceptual — use the terminal. "Which of these wizard layouts feels right?" is visual — use the browser.
    26→
    27→## How It Works
    28→
    29→The server watches a directory for HTML files and serves the newest one to the browser. You write HTML content, the user sees it in their browser and can click to select options. Selections are recorded to a `.events` file that you read on your next turn.
    30→
    31→**Content fragments vs full documents:** If your HTML file starts with `<!DOCTYPE` or `<html`, the server serves it as-is (just injects the helper script). Otherwise, the server automatically wraps your content in the frame template — adding the header, CSS theme, selection indicator, and all interactive infrastructure. **Write content fragments by default.** Only write full documents when you need complete control over the page.
    32→
    33→## Starting a Session
    34→
    35→```bash
    36→# Start server with persistence (mockups saved to project)
    37→scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project
    38→
    39→# Returns: {"type":"server-started","port":52341,"url":"http://localhost:52341",
    40→#           "screen_dir":"/path/to/project/.superpowers/brainstorm/12345-1706000000"}
    41→```
    42→
    43→Save `screen_dir` from the response. Tell user to open the URL.
    44→
    45→**Finding connection info:** The server writes its startup JSON to `$SCREEN_DIR/.server-info`. If you launched the server in the background and didn't capture stdout, read that file to get the URL and port. When using `--project-dir`, check `<project>/.superpowers/brainstorm/` for the session directory.
    46→
    47→**Note:** Pass the project root as `--project-dir` so mockups persist in `.superpowers/brainstorm/` and survive server restarts. Without it, files go to `/tmp` and get cleaned up. Remind the user to add `.superpowers/` to `.gitignore` if it's not already there.
    48→
    49→**Launching the server by platform:**
    50→
    51→**Claude Code (macOS / Linux):**
    52→```bash
    53→# Default mode works — the script backgrounds the server itself
    54→scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project
    55→```
    56→
    57→**Claude Code (Windows):**
    58→```bash
    59→# Windows auto-detects and uses foreground mode, which blocks the tool call.
    60→# Use run_in_background: true on the Bash tool call so the server survives
    61→# across conversation turns.
    62→scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project
    63→```
    64→When calling this via the Bash tool, set `run_in_background: true`. Then read `$SCREEN_DIR/.server-info` on the next turn to get the URL and port.
    65→
    66→**Codex:**
    67→```bash
    68→# Codex reaps background processes. The script auto-detects CODEX_CI and
    69→# switches to foreground mode. Run it normally — no extra flags needed.
    70→scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project
    71→```
    72→
    73→**Gemini CLI:**
    74→```bash
    75→# Use --foreground and set is_background: true on your shell tool call
    76→# so the process survives across turns
    77→scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project --foreground
    78→```
    79→
    80→**Other environments:** The server must keep running in the background across conversation turns. If your environment reaps detached processes, use `--foreground` and launch the command with your platform's background execution mechanism.
    81→
    82→If the URL is unreachable from your browser (common in remote/containerized setups), bind a non-loopback host:
    83→
    84→```bash
    85→scripts/start-server.sh \
    86→  --project-dir /path/to/project \
    87→  --host 0.0.0.0 \
    88→  --url-host localhost
    89→```
    90→
    91→Use `--url-host` to control what hostname is printed in the returned URL JSON.
    92→
    93→## The Loop
    94→
    95→1. **Check server is alive**, then **write HTML** to a new file in `screen_dir`:
    96→   - Before each write, check that `$SCREEN_DIR/.server-info` exists. If it doesn't (or `.server-stopped` exists), the server has shut down — restart it with `start-server.sh` before continuing. The server auto-exits after 30 minutes of inactivity.
    97→   - Use semantic filenames: `platform.html`, `visual-style.html`, `layout.html`
    98→   - **Never reuse filenames** — each screen gets a fresh file
    99→   - Use Write tool — **never use cat/heredoc** (dumps noise into terminal)
   100→   - Server automatically serves the newest file
   101→
   102→2. **Tell user what to expect and end your turn:**
   103→   - Remind them of the URL (every step, not just first)
   104→   - Give a brief text summary of what's on screen (e.g., "Showing 3 layout options for the homepage")
   105→   - Ask them to respond in the terminal: "Take a look and let me know what you think. Click to select an option if you'd like."
   106→
   107→3. **On your next turn** — after the user responds in the terminal:
   108→   - Read `$SCREEN_DIR/.events` if it exists — this contains the user's browser interactions (clicks, selections) as JSON lines
   109→   - Merge with the user's terminal text to get the full picture
   110→   - The terminal message is the primary feedback; `.events` provides structured interaction data
   111→
   112→4. **Iterate or advance** — if feedback changes current screen, write a new file (e.g., `layout-v2.html`). Only move to the next question when the current step is validated.
   113→
   114→5. **Unload when returning to terminal** — when the next step doesn't need the browser (e.g., a clarifying question, a tradeoff discussion), push a waiting screen to clear the stale content:
   115→
   116→   ```html
   117→   <!-- filename: waiting.html (or waiting-2.html, etc.) -->
   118→   <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;min-height:60vh">
   119→     <p class="subtitle">Continuing in terminal...</p>
   120→   </div>
   121→   ```
   122→
   123→   This prevents the user from staring at a resolved choice while the conversation has moved on. When the next visual question comes up, push a new content file as usual.
   124→
   125→6. Repeat until done.
   126→
   127→## Writing Content Fragments
   128→
   129→Write just the content that goes inside the page. The server wraps it in the frame template automatically (header, theme CSS, selection indicator, and all interactive infrastructure).
   130→
   131→**Minimal example:**
   132→
   133→```html
   134→<h2>Which layout works better?</h2>
   135→<p class="subtitle">Consider readability and visual hierarchy</p>
   136→
   137→<div class="options">
   138→  <div class="option" data-choice="a" onclick="toggleSelect(this)">
   139→    <div class="letter">A</div>
   140→    <div class="content">
   141→      <h3>Single Column</h3>
   142→      <p>Clean, focused reading experience</p>
   143→    </div>
   144→  </div>
   145→  <div class="option" data-choice="b" onclick="toggleSelect(this)">
   146→    <div class="letter">B</div>
   147→    <div class="content">
   148→      <h3>Two Column</h3>
   149→      <p>Sidebar navigation with main content</p>
   150→    </div>
   151→  </div>
   152→</div>
   153→```
   154→
   155→That's it. No `<html>`, no CSS, no `<script>` tags needed. The server provides all of that.
   156→
   157→## CSS Classes Available
   158→
   159→The frame template provides these CSS classes for your content:
   160→
   161→### Options (A/B/C choices)
   162→
   163→```html
   164→<div class="options">
   165→  <div class="option" data-choice="a" onclick="toggleSelect(this)">
   166→    <div class="letter">A</div>
   167→    <div class="content">
   168→      <h3>Title</h3>
   169→      <p>Description</p>
   170→    </div>
   171→  </div>
   172→</div>
   173→```
   174→
   175→**Multi-select:** Add `data-multiselect` to the container to let users select multiple options. Each click toggles the item. The indicator bar shows the count.
   176→
   177→```html
   178→<div class="options" data-multiselect>
   179→  <!-- same option markup — users can select/deselect multiple -->
   180→</div>
   181→```
   182→
   183→### Cards (visual designs)
   184→
   185→```html
   186→<div class="cards">
   187→  <div class="card" data-choice="design1" onclick="toggleSelect(this)">
   188→    <div class="card-image"><!-- mockup content --></div>
   189→    <div class="card-body">
   190→      <h3>Name</h3>
   191→      <p>Description</p>
   192→    </div>
   193→  </div>
   194→</div>
   195→```
   196→
   197→### Mockup container
   198→
   199→```html
   200→<div class="mockup">
   201→  <div class="mockup-header">Preview: Dashboard Layout</div>
   202→  <div class="mockup-body"><!-- your mockup HTML --></div>
   203→</div>
   204→```
   205→
   206→### Split view (side-by-side)
   207→
   208→```html
   209→<div class="split">
   210→  <div class="mockup"><!-- left --></div>
   211→  <div class="mockup"><!-- right --></div>
   212→</div>
   213→```
   214→
   215→### Pros/Cons
   216→
   217→```html
   218→<div class="pros-cons">
   219→  <div class="pros"><h4>Pros</h4><ul><li>Benefit</li></ul></div>
   220→  <div class="cons"><h4>Cons</h4><ul><li>Drawback</li></ul></div>
   221→</div>
   222→```
   223→
   224→### Mock elements (wireframe building blocks)
   225→
   226→```html
   227→<div class="mock-nav">Logo | Home | About | Contact</div>
   228→<div style="display: flex;">
   229→  <div class="mock-sidebar">Navigation</div>
   230→  <div class="mock-content">Main content area</div>
   231→</div>
   232→<button class="mock-button">Action Button</button>
   233→<input class="mock-input" placeholder="Input field">
   234→<div class="placeholder">Placeholder area</div>
   235→```
   236→
   237→### Typography and sections
   238→
   239→- `h2` — page title
   240→- `h3` — section heading
   241→- `.subtitle` — secondary text below title
   242→- `.section` — content block with bottom margin
   243→- `.label` — small uppercase label text
   244→
   245→## Browser Events Format
   246→
   247→When the user clicks options in the browser, their interactions are recorded to `$SCREEN_DIR/.events` (one JSON object per line). The file is cleared automatically when you push a new screen.
   248→
   249→```jsonl
   250→{"type":"click","choice":"a","text":"Option A - Simple Layout","timestamp":1706000101}
   251→{"type":"click","choice":"c","text":"Option C - Complex Grid","timestamp":1706000108}
   252→{"type":"click","choice":"b","text":"Option B - Hybrid","timestamp":1706000115}
   253→```
   254→
   255→The full event stream shows the user's exploration path — they may click multiple options before settling. The last `choice` event is typically the final selection, but the pattern of clicks can reveal hesitation or preferences worth asking about.
   256→
   257→If `.events` doesn't exist, the user didn't interact with the browser — use only their terminal text.
   258→
   259→## Design Tips
   260→
   261→- **Scale fidelity to the question** — wireframes for layout, polish for polish questions
   262→- **Explain the question on each page** — "Which layout feels more professional?" not just "Pick one"
   263→- **Iterate before advancing** — if feedback changes current screen, write a new version
   264→- **2-4 options max** per screen
   265→- **Use real content when it matters** — for a photography portfolio, use actual images (Unsplash). Placeholder content obscures design issues.
   266→- **Keep mockups simple** — focus on layout and structure, not pixel-perfect design
   267→
   268→## File Naming
   269→
   270→- Use semantic names: `platform.html`, `visual-style.html`, `layout.html`
   271→- Never reuse filenames — each screen must be a new file
   272→- For iterations: append version suffix like `layout-v2.html`, `layout-v3.html`
   273→- Server serves newest file by modification time
   274→
   275→## Cleaning Up
   276→
   277→```bash
   278→scripts/stop-server.sh $SCREEN_DIR
   279→```
   280→
   281→If the session used `--project-dir`, mockup files persist in `.superpowers/brainstorm/` for later reference. Only `/tmp` sessions get deleted on stop.
   282→
   283→## Reference
   284→
   285→- Frame template (CSS reference): `scripts/frame-template.html`
   286→- Helper script (client-side): `scripts/helper.js`
   287→
```

> AGENT

Visual companion is ready. Open **http://localhost:57262** in your browser.

Now, before I dive into design suggestions, let me ask the first clarifying question. Since this is a conceptual scope question, I'll keep it in the terminal:

**What's your primary design philosophy goal?**

The iOS app achieves its look through SwiftUI's native iOS design language — system fonts, native list styles, platform-standard navigation patterns. On Android, there are a few different paths to "pretty":

- **A) iOS-faithful port** — Replicate the iOS visual design as closely as possible on Android (same spacing, colors, layout structure, even if it feels non-native to Android users)
- **B) Native Android beauty** — Use Material 3 but heavily customize it (custom color scheme, typography, component shapes) to feel polished and premium while still feeling "Android-native"
- **C) Platform-agnostic custom design** — Create a unique visual identity for Hackers'Pub that looks great on both platforms but doesn't strictly follow either platform's conventions (think: Telegram, Discord approach)

Which direction appeals to you most?

> DEVELOPER

C

> AGENT

Great choice — a custom Hackers'Pub identity that feels polished regardless of platform.

**Are there any apps (besides the iOS version) whose visual style you admire?** For example:

- **Telegram** — clean, flat, fast-feeling
- **Discord** — dark-first, card-heavy, playful
- **Ivory/Ice Cubes** (Mastodon clients) — editorial, typography-focused
- **Bear/iA Writer** — minimalist, writing-oriented
- **Something else?**

This will help me understand the visual mood you're drawn to. You can pick multiple or describe the vibe in your own words.

> DEVELOPER

Ivory compatible please

> AGENT

Ivory — excellent taste. That's a very clear design direction: editorial, refined, typography-first with subtle warmth and premium feel.

Let me put together some visual options for you. I'll start with the most important screen — the timeline/post card — since that sets the tone for everything else.

> AGENT

## Complete Design — Ivory-inspired Hackers'Pub Android

### Design System

**Color Palette (Light):**
- Background: `#FFFFFF`
- Primary text: `#1C1C1E`
- Secondary text: `#AEAEB2`
- Accent (interactive): `#44403C` (Deep Stone) — links, mentions, hashtags use font-weight instead of color contrast
- Dividers: `#F2F2F7`
- Semantic: red `#E8453C` (reactions), green `#34D399` (shares), red dot for notifications

**Color Palette (Dark):**
- Background: `#171717`
- Primary text: `#FAFAFA`
- Secondary text: `#737373`
- Accent: `#D6D3D1` (inverted stone)
- Dividers: `#262626`
- Same semantic colors

**Typography:** System default (Roboto), emphasis via weight (400/600/700) not color. Large title 22sp bold, body 15sp, captions 13sp.

**Spacing:** 16dp standard padding, 12dp between elements, 8dp fine spacing. Consistent throughout.

**Shapes:** Circular avatars (42dp timeline, 80dp profile), 20dp pill buttons, no card elevation.

### Screen-by-Screen

**Timeline:** Large bold title top-left, avatar + compose button top-right. Posts separated by hairline dividers. Each post: avatar, name (bold) + timestamp, body text, hashtags (weight-emphasized), engagement row (icon + count, muted until active).

**Profile:** Back arrow + ellipsis menu in header. Centered 80dp avatar, name, handle, bio, stats row (Posts/Followers/Following), Follow + Share buttons as pills. "Follows you" as subtle capsule tag. Posts below the divider.

**Notifications:** Same large title header. Notification rows with actor avatar, action description, timestamp. Grouped by type.

**Explore:** Large title "Explore", segmented tabs (Local/Global) below header. Same post rendering as timeline.

**Search:** Search bar at top, results as post cards or actor rows.

**Compose:** Navigation bar with Cancel/Post. Markdown editor, reply-to indicator if replying. Visibility picker as segmented control.

**Settings:** Grouped list sections (similar to iOS Settings style). Font size slider, toggles, app info.

**Sign-in:** Centered layout — logo, title, text fields with rounded borders, primary stone button.

**Bottom Tab Bar:** 4 tabs with labeled icons — Home, Explore, Alerts (with red notification dot), Search. Active tab filled icon + stone color, inactive outlined + muted.

---

Does this complete design look right to you? Anything you'd like to adjust before I write the formal spec?

  "content": "# Hackers'Pub Android — Full UI Redesign\n\n**Date:** 2026-03-19\n**Status:** Approved\n**Goal:** Replace the default Material Design 3 styling with a custom, Ivory-inspired design system that gives Hackers'Pub a distinctive, editorial identity across both light and dark themes.\n\n## Design Philosophy\n\nPlatform-agnostic custom identity — not Material, not iOS-native, but a unique Hackers'Pub look inspired by Ivory (Tapbots' Mastodon client). Near-monochrome palette with Deep Stone accent. Typography-first: font weight carries emphasis rather than color. Semantic colors (red, green) reserved for interactive state changes, giving them maximum impact against the neutral base.\n\n## Design System\n\n### Color Palette\n\n#### Light Theme\n| Token | Value | Usage |\n|-------|-------|-------|\n| `background` | `#FFFFFF` | Screen background |\n| `surface` | `#F2F2F7` | Dividers, secondary surfaces, icon button backgrounds |\n| `textPrimary` | `#1C1C1E` | Headlines, names, primary content |\n| `textBody` | `#3A3A3C` | Body text, post content |\n| `textSecondary` | `#AEAEB2` | Handles, timestamps, inactive tab labels |\n| `accent` | `#44403C` | Active tab icons/labels, links (via weight), follow buttons, interactive elements |\n| `accentMuted` | `#78716C` | Hashtags, relationship tags background at 10% opacity |\n| `divider` | `#F2F2F7` | Hairline separators between posts |\n| `buttonOutline` | `#D6D3D1` | Secondary button borders |\n| `error` / `reaction` | `#E8453C` | Heart reactions, error states, notification dots |\n| `share` | `#34D399` | Share/repost active state |\n\n#### Dark Theme\n| Token | Value | Usage |\n|-------|-------|-------|\n| `background` | `#171717` | Screen background |\n| `surface` | `#262626` | Dividers, secondary surfaces, icon button backgrounds |\n| `textPrimary` | `#FAFAFA` | Headlines, names |\n| `textBody` | `#D4D4D4` | Body text |\n| `textSecondary` | `#737373` | Handles, timestamps, inactive tabs |\n| `accent` | `#D6D3D1` | Active tab icons/labels, interactive elements (inverted stone) |\n| `accentMuted` | `#A8A29E` | Hashtags, tags |\n| `divider` | `#262626` | Hairline separators |\n| `buttonOutline` | `#525252` | Secondary button borders |\n| `error` / `reaction` | `#E8453C` | Same as light |\n| `share` | `#34D399` | Same as light |\n\n### Typography\n\nSystem default (Roboto on Android). No custom fonts — emphasis via weight hierarchy only.\n\n| Style | Size | Weight | Usage |\n|-------|------|--------|-------|\n| `titleLarge` | 22sp | 700 (Bold) | Screen titles (Timeline, Explore, etc.) |\n| `titleMedium` | 20sp | 700 (Bold) | Profile name |\n| `bodyLarge` | 15sp | 400 (Regular) | Post content, bio text |\n| `bodyMedium` | 14sp | 400 (Regular) | Stats labels, settings items |\n| `labelMedium` | 13sp | 400 (Regular) | Handles, timestamps, engagement counts |\n| `labelSmall` | 12sp | 500 (Medium) | Hashtags, relationship tags |\n| `caption` | 11sp | 500 (Medium) | Tag pills, repost indicators |\n| `tabLabel` | 10sp | 600 (SemiBold) | Bottom tab labels |\n\n**Letter spacing:** -0.3sp on `titleLarge` for tighter headline feel. Default elsewhere.\n\n### Spacing\n\nBase unit: 4dp. Consistent throughout.\n\n| Token | Value | Usage |\n|-------|-------|-------|\n| `xs` | 4dp | Fine spacing (between name and handle) |\n| `sm` | 8dp | Compact spacing (engagement icon gaps) |\n| `md` | 12dp | Element spacing (avatar-to-text gap, between post sections) |\n| `lg` | 16dp | Standard padding (screen edges, post padding) |\n| `xl` | 24dp | Section spacing (stats row gaps) |\n\n### Shapes\n\n| Element | Radius | Size |\n|---------|--------|------|\n| Avatar (timeline) | Circle | 42dp |\n| Avatar (profile) | Circle | 80dp |\n| Avatar (repost indicator) | Circle | 16dp |\n| Pill buttons (Follow, etc.) | 20dp | Height ~36dp |\n| Icon buttons (compose, etc.) | Circle | 28dp |\n| Relationship tags | Full capsule | Auto-width |\n| Notification dot | Circle | 8dp |\n\nNo card elevation or shadows anywhere. Post separation via hairline dividers only.\n\n### Icons\n\nOutlined stroke icons (Feather/Lucide style), 22dp for tab bar, 16dp for engagement bar. Stroke width 1.5dp.\n\n- Active state: filled icon with accent color\n- Inactive state: outlined icon with secondary color\n- Engagement active states: filled with semantic color (red heart, green repost)\n\n## Screen Specifications\n\n### Timeline Screen\n\n**Header:**\n- Large bold title \"Timeline\" (22sp/700) top-left\n- Right side: compose button (28dp circle, surface background, + icon) and user avatar (28dp circle)\n- No border below header — content starts immediately\n\n**Content:**\n- Vertical scrolling list (LazyColumn)\n- Posts separated by 1dp hairline dividers (`divider` color) with 16dp horizontal margin\n- Pull-to-refresh support\n\n**Post Card Layout:**\n- 16dp horizontal padding, 12dp vertical padding\n- Repost indicator (if shared): 16dp avatar + repost icon + actor name, caption size, secondary color, indented to align with post content (48dp left padding)\n- Author row: 42dp circular avatar, 12dp gap, then name (15sp/600) + timestamp (13sp/secondary) on same baseline\n- Body text: 15sp/400, `textBody` color, 4dp below author row\n- Hashtags: 11sp/500, `accentMuted` color, 6dp below body\n- Engagement bar: 10dp below content, icons (16dp) + counts (13sp), 18dp gaps between items. Muted by default, semantic colors when active (red heart filled, green repost)\n\n**Quoted Post (if present):**\n- Indented block below body text\n- 1dp border in `divider` color, 8dp border-radius, 8dp padding\n- Smaller avatar (32dp), author info, truncated content\n\n**Floating Action Button:** None — compose is in the header\n\n### Profile Screen\n\n**Header:** Back arrow (left) + ellipsis menu (right), no title text\n\n**Profile Section (centered):**\n- 80dp circular avatar\n- Name: 20sp/700, 12dp below avatar\n- Handle: 14sp/400, `textSecondary`, 2dp below name\n- Bio: 14sp/400, `textBody`, centered, 10dp below handle, 24dp horizontal margin\n- Stats row: 24dp gap between items, centered, 14dp below bio\n  - Each stat: count (16sp/700) above label (12sp/secondary)\n  - Three items: Posts, Followers, Following\n- Action buttons: 16dp below stats, centered\n  - Follow: pill button, `accent` background, white text, 28dp horizontal padding\n  - Share: pill button, transparent, `buttonOutline` border, share icon only\n- Relationship tag: \"Follows you\" capsule, `accentMuted` at 10% opacity background, 11sp/500, 10dp below buttons\n\n**Content:** Hairline divider, then post list identical to timeline (without avatars if showing own profile — keep avatars for consistency)\n\n### Notifications Screen\n\n**Header:** Large bold title \"Notifications\" (same as timeline)\n\n**Notification Rows:**\n- 42dp actor avatar (circular), 12dp gap\n- Action text: actor name (bold) + action description + timestamp\n- Notification type indicated by subtle icon or text (\"liked your post\", \"shared your post\", \"followed you\")\n- Hairline dividers between items\n- Unread indicator: red dot (8dp) on the Alerts tab icon\n- Empty state: centered text \"No notifications yet\"\n\n### Explore Screen\n\n**Header:** Large bold title \"Explore\"\n\n**Segmented Tabs:** Below header, \"Local\" / \"Global\" segmented control\n- Active tab: `accent` text, underline indicator\n- Inactive tab: `textSecondary`\n\n**Content:** Same post list as timeline, scoped to local or global feed\n\n### Search Screen\n\n**Header:** Large bold title \"Search\"\n\n**Search Bar:** Rounded text field below header, `surface` background, 12dp border-radius, search icon left-aligned, 16dp horizontal margin\n\n**Results:** Actor rows (avatar + name + handle) and post cards, depending on result type\n\n### Compose Screen\n\n**Header:** Navigation bar style\n- Left: \"Cancel\" text button (`accent` color)\n- Right: \"Post\" pill button (`accent` background, white text), disabled state at 40% opacity\n- Title: \"New Post\" or \"Reply\" centered (17sp/600)\n\n**Content:**\n- Reply indicator (if replying): \"Replying to @handle\" in `textSecondary`, 13sp\n- Text editor: full-width, 15sp, placeholder \"What's on your mind?\"\n- Markdown support with live preview toggle\n\n**Bottom toolbar:** Visibility selector, language picker — subtle icons in `textSecondary`\n\n### Settings Screen\n\n**Header:** Large bold title \"Settings\"\n\n**Content:** Grouped list sections with section headers (13sp/500, uppercase, `textSecondary`)\n\nSections:\n- **App Info:** Logo (80dp, 16dp corner radius), app name, version\n- **Typography:** Font size slider (0.75x–2.0x)\n- **Timeline:** Markdown truncation options\n- **Engagement:** Confirmation toggles\n- **Links:** In-app browser toggle\n- **App Icons:** Alternative icon selection\n\nEach row: standard list item, 16dp padding, `textPrimary` label, `textSecondary` value/description. Hairline dividers between rows.\n\n### Sign-In Screen\n\n**Layout:** Centered vertically\n\n- Logo: 120dp, centered\n- Title: \"Hackers' Pub\" (22sp/700), 16dp below logo\n- Subtitle: \"Sign in to continue\" (14sp, `textSecondary`), 4dp below title\n- Username field: rounded border (12dp radius), `surface` background, 32dp below subtitle, 32dp horizontal margin\n- Verification code field: same style (appears after username submission)\n- Primary button: pill shape, `accent` background, white text, full width within margins\n- Error text: `error` color, 13sp, below button\n\n## Migration Strategy\n\nThis redesign replaces the existing Material Design 3 theming while keeping the same Compose architecture. Changes are purely in the UI layer:\n\n1. **Theme.kt** — Replace M3 color scheme with custom color tokens\n2. **Spacing.kt** — Already exists, minor adjustments to values\n3. **New: Typography.kt** — Define custom text styles (not M3 Typography)\n4. **New: Shapes.kt** — Define shape tokens\n5. **New: Icons.kt** — Custom icon set (if switching from Material Icons to Feather/Lucide)\n6. **Each screen** — Replace M3 components (TopAppBar, FloatingActionButton, etc.) with custom composables\n7. **PostCard** — Restyle engagement bar, remove card elevation, update spacing\n8. **CompactTopBar** — Replace with new LargeTitle header composable\n\nNo changes to ViewModels, repositories, data layer, or navigation graph. Pure visual transformation.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- Custom animations/transitions (can be added later)\n- Custom font families (using system Roboto)\n- Tablet/landscape layouts\n- Widget design\n- Onboarding flow\n"
}

# Design Specification Review: Hackers'Pub Android UI Redesign

**Spec:** `/Users/kodingwarrior/fediverse/hackerspub-android/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-03-19-android-redesign-design.md`

**Reviewer's summary:** The spec is well-structured and provides a solid foundation for the redesign. It covers the primary design tokens, screen layouts, and migration strategy. However, there are several gaps in completeness, ambiguous dimensions, missing screen coverage, and inconsistencies between the spec's design system and the spec's own screen descriptions that would cause developer confusion during implementation.

---

## BLOCKERS (Must fix before implementation)

### B1. Post Detail Screen is completely absent from the spec

The codebase contains a full `PostDetailScreen` at `/Users/kodingwarrior/fediverse/hackerspub-android/app/src/main/java/pub/hackers/android/ui/screens/postdetail/PostDetailScreen.kt` which is one of the most complex screens in the app. It includes reply target previews, full engagement stats, reaction groups with a reaction picker bottom sheet, shares/quotes bottom sheets, post deletion, visibility indicators, and a reply FAB. The spec does not mention this screen at all. A developer would have no guidance on how to restyle it, which is especially problematic because PostDetail's layout differs significantly from PostCard (expanded view with timestamp, full stats row, action bar).

### B2. Bottom navigation bar is not specified

The spec describes individual screens but never specifies the bottom navigation bar itself. The existing codebase in `/Users/kodingwarrior/fediverse/hackerspub-android/app/src/main/java/pub/hackers/android/ui/HackersPubApp.kt` has five tabs (Timeline, Notifications, Explore, Search, Settings) using M3 `NavigationBar`. The spec mentions tab labels at 10sp/600 and icon sizing at 22dp, but there is no dedicated section describing the bottom bar's overall height, background color, selected/unselected icon colors, divider line above the bar, or how the tab labels relate to the icons. The icons section says "Active state: filled icon with accent color" and "Inactive state: outlined icon with secondary color", but this guidance is scattered and needs to be consolidated into a bottom bar specification.

### B3. No specification for the `textBody` vs `textPrimary` color for author names in PostCard

The spec says author names in the post card should be "15sp/600" but never assigns a color. The typography table defines `bodyLarge` as 15sp/400, not 15sp/600. The author name weight (600 = SemiBold) does not map to any defined typography style. This is a contradiction: should the developer create a new style, or should they use `bodyLarge` with a manual weight override? The spec needs to either define a `bodyLargeSemiBold` variant or clarify that weight overrides are expected inline.

### B4. Engagement bar semantic colors are incomplete

The spec defines `error`/`reaction` as `#E8453C` for "Heart reactions" and `share` as `#34D399` for "Share/repost active state". However, the existing codebase's engagement bar has five actions: reply, share, reaction (heart), quote, and external share. The spec says engagement icons are "Muted by default, semantic colors when active (red heart filled, green repost)" but does not define:
- What color the reply icon uses when active
- What color the quote icon uses when active
- Whether the external share button changes state at all

This ambiguity would force the developer to make design decisions that should be in the spec.

---

## WARNINGS (Should fix but not blocking)

### W1. Missing `xxl` spacing token usage

The existing `Spacing.kt` defines `xxl = 32.dp` which is used in the codebase (e.g., `32.dp` horizontal margin on sign-in screen). The spec's spacing table only goes up to `xl = 24dp`. The sign-in spec itself says "32dp horizontal margin" and "32dp below subtitle", confirming the need for this value, but the design system does not include it.

### W2. Avatar size discrepancy between spec and codebase

The spec defines timeline avatars as 42dp, but the existing PostCard uses 48dp (`Modifier.size(48.dp)`). The spec defines profile avatars as 80dp, but the existing ProfileHeader uses 96dp. These are intentional design changes, but worth calling out as the spec does not mention this as a deliberate reduction, which may cause confusion about whether it is a mistake or intentional.

### W3. Quoted post spec conflicts with codebase quoted post avatar size

The spec says quoted posts should have a "Smaller avatar (32dp)" but the existing `QuotedPostPreview` uses 20dp. The spec's 32dp would be larger than what exists. This should be confirmed as intentional.

### W4. Profile screen is missing follower/following/post counts specification details

The spec says "Stats row: count (16sp/700) above label (12sp/secondary)" but the existing `ProfileResult` model in `/Users/kodingwarrior/fediverse/hackerspub-android/app/src/main/java/pub/hackers/android/domain/model/Models.kt` does not contain follower, following, or post counts. The `Actor` model has no stats fields. Either the data layer needs to be extended (violating the "No changes to ViewModels, repositories, data layer" promise in the Migration Strategy), or the spec needs to acknowledge this is an existing limitation and the stats row should be omitted or noted as a future addition.

### W5. Compose screen title spec is inconsistent with codebase

The spec says the compose title should be "New Post" or "Reply" centered at 17sp/600. However: (a) 17sp is not in the typography table at all, (b) the existing codebase uses three possible titles: "Compose", "Reply", and "Quoting". The spec omits the quoting state entirely.

### W6. No specification for media grid appearance in the new design

The codebase has a full `MediaGrid` composable with 1-image, 2-image, and 3+ image layouts using `RoundedCornerShape(12.dp)`. The spec mentions no media-related styling: no image corner radius, no media aspect ratios, no media container behavior. Since the spec says "No card elevation or shadows anywhere", it should clarify whether media images retain their current rounded corners or adopt a different treatment.

### W7. Notification colors in codebase are hardcoded, not in spec

The existing `NotificationsScreen` uses distinct hardcoded colors for each notification type: green for follow (`#4CAF50`), blue for mention (`#2196F3`), purple for reply (`#9C27B0`), orange for quote (`#FF9800`), cyan for share (`#00BCD4`), pink for react (`#E91E63`). The spec's design philosophy says "near-monochrome palette" with "semantic colors reserved for interactive state changes", but does not specify what colors notification type icons should use. Should they all become `textSecondary`? Should they retain distinct colors? This needs explicit guidance.

### W8. Missing specification for Article type differentiation

The codebase has special handling for `typename == "Article"` in both PostCard (shows an "Article" badge) and PostDetailScreen (uses `headlineLarge` for the title). The spec does not mention article-specific styling at all.

### W9. Font size scaling feature is not addressed

The Settings screen and `HackersPubApp` implement a font size scaling feature (0.75x-2.0x via `LocalFontScale`). The spec's Settings section mentions "Font size slider (0.75x-2.0x)" but the design system's typography section does not address how the custom text styles interact with this scaling mechanism. The spec should clarify whether the custom typography definitions serve as base values that get scaled.

### W10. Repost indicator layout description is ambiguous

The spec says the repost indicator should be "indented to align with post content (48dp left padding)". But the timeline avatar is defined as 42dp with a 12dp gap. That means post content starts at 42+12 = 54dp from the left edge. The 48dp indent would not align with the content. This arithmetic should be reconciled.

---

## NOTES (Nice to have improvements)

### N1. Missing dark theme contrast ratios / accessibility verification

The spec provides exact hex values but does not mention WCAG contrast ratio compliance. For example, `textSecondary` in dark theme is `#737373` on `background` `#171717`. This is a 4.48:1 ratio, which passes AA for normal text but fails AAA. The `textBody` in light theme (`#3A3A3C` on `#FFFFFF`) is 9.73:1, which is excellent. A note confirming accessibility compliance would strengthen the spec.

### N2. No specification for transition or animation between states

The spec's "Out of Scope" section says "Custom animations/transitions (can be added later)". This is fine, but it would help to note whether the current M3 default transitions (e.g., pull-to-refresh animation, bottom sheet entrance) should be preserved or if the developer should use `AnimationSpec.None` to strip them.

### N3. No mention of shimmer/skeleton loading states

The codebase uses a simple `CircularProgressIndicator` for all loading states. If the redesign should introduce shimmer placeholders (common in Ivory-style apps), this should be noted. If not, explicitly stating "Continue using spinner-based loading" prevents scope creep.

### N4. Missing spec for the ReactionPicker bottom sheet

The existing codebase has a `ReactionPicker` component shown in a `ModalBottomSheet` from the PostDetailScreen. This is a non-trivial UI element with emoji selection and loading state. No styling guidance is provided.

### N5. Search bar spec says 12dp border-radius but existing Spacing/Radius system has `xl = 12.dp`

The spec should reference its own design tokens by name rather than raw values to maintain consistency. For example, "Search bar border-radius: `Radius.xl` (12dp)" instead of just "12dp border-radius".

### N6. Settings "App Icons" section is specified but not in codebase

The spec lists "App Icons: Alternative icon selection" as a settings section, but the current SettingsScreen has no such feature. This should be marked as "new feature" or removed from the redesign spec since the document claims "No changes to ViewModels, repositories, data layer."

### N7. The `Radius` object in Spacing.kt is not aligned with spec shapes

The existing `Radius` object defines `sm=4`, `md=6`, `lg=8`, `xl=12`. The spec's shapes table defines pill buttons at 20dp, icon buttons as circles, quoted posts at 8dp. The spec should provide an updated Radius token table, or at minimum reference which Radius tokens map to which UI elements.

### N8. Divider horizontal margin inconsistency

The spec says dividers have "16dp horizontal margin" but the existing codebase uses full-bleed `HorizontalDivider()` calls with no horizontal padding. The spec should clarify whether this is a deliberate change requiring `Modifier.padding(horizontal = 16.dp)` on every divider.

### N9. Icon library migration not fully specified

The spec says "Feather/Lucide style" icons and lists a new file `Icons.kt` in the migration strategy. However, it does not provide a mapping from the current Material Icons (used extensively: `Icons.Filled.Repeat`, `Icons.Outlined.ChatBubbleOutline`, `Icons.Filled.Favorite`, etc.) to specific Feather/Lucide icon names. Without this mapping, the developer will need to make subjective icon choices for every icon in the app.

### N10. No guidance on handle display format

The codebase shows handles as `@handle` in most places. The spec mentions "Handle: 14sp/400, `textSecondary`" for the profile screen but does not specify whether the `@` prefix is part of the display format or handled by the composable. This is minor but worth standardizing.

---

## Summary

| Category | Count |
|----------|-------|
| BLOCKER  | 4     |
| WARNING  | 10    |
| NOTE     | 10    |

**Key actions required before implementation begins:**

1. Add a Post Detail Screen section to the spec (B1)
2. Add a Bottom Navigation Bar section to the spec (B2)
3. Resolve the author name typography conflict at 15sp/600 (B3)
4. Define engagement icon active colors for all five actions (B4)

The spec is strongest in its color palette and spacing definitions, and the migration strategy is pragmatic. The main risk is that a developer implementing this spec would need to make too many unsupported design decisions for uncovered screens and states, particularly PostDetail (the most complex screen in the app) which is entirely absent.

I now have a thorough understanding of both the design spec and the existing codebase. Let me verify the blocker fixes systematically.

---

**APPROVED**

All four previously identified blockers have been adequately resolved:

**B1: Post Detail Screen -- RESOLVED.** The spec now includes a comprehensive "Post Detail Screen" section (lines 153-200) covering the header, expanded post content, article post variant, engagement stats row, action bar, reaction groups, reaction picker bottom sheet, shares/quotes list bottom sheet, and reply thread. This aligns well with the existing `PostDetailScreen.kt` implementation and provides sufficient detail for the redesign.

**B2: Bottom Navigation Bar -- RESOLVED.** A dedicated "Bottom Navigation Bar" section (lines 103-114) now specifies height (56dp), background color per theme, top border treatment, tab sets for both authenticated and unauthenticated states, icon sizing, label typography, active/inactive styling, and notification dot placement. This maps cleanly to the existing `HackersPubApp.kt` navigation setup.

**B3: Author name typography conflict -- RESOLVED.** The `bodyLargeSemiBold` style (line 53) is explicitly defined at 15sp/600, distinct from `bodyLarge` at 15sp/400. The post card layout correctly references `bodyLargeSemiBold` for author names (line 141), eliminating the ambiguity.

**B4: Engagement active colors incomplete -- RESOLVED.** All five engagement actions are individually specified (lines 97-101): Reply uses `accent`, Share/Repost uses `share` green, Reaction/Heart uses `reaction` red (filled), Quote uses `accent`, and External Share has no active state (always `textSecondary`). This is complete and unambiguous.

**No new blockers introduced.** The spec is internally consistent, maps to existing screens and navigation structure, and the migration strategy (lines 293-306) correctly identifies it as a UI-layer-only change. The notes about profile stats availability (line 308) and font scaling compatibility (line 310) are pragmatic safeguards. The tab configuration in the spec (4 tabs authenticated, 4 tabs unauthenticated -- line 108-109) differs from the current implementation (5 tabs authenticated with Settings, 3 tabs unauthenticated) but this is an intentional redesign choice, not an error -- Settings moves elsewhere or is accessed differently.

### SUMMARY OF KEY FINDINGS

**Global Theme Colors Used Throughout:**
- Primary: `#262626` (light mode) / `#FAFAFA` (dark mode)
- Surface/Background: `#FFFFFF` (light) / `#262626` (dark)
- Accent colors: Blues (`#2563EB`, `#60A5FA`), reds for errors
- Notification-specific colors: 6 distinct colors for different notification types

**Material3 Components Most Used:**
1. `Scaffold` - Every screen
2. `Text` - Ubiquitous
3. `Icon` / `IconButton` - Throughout
4. `Card` / `Surface` - Content containers
5. `NavigationBar` / `Tab` - Navigation
6. `FloatingActionButton` - Call-to-action
7. `MaterialTheme` references - Color and typography

**Spacing System:**
- Consistent use of 4dp base unit (xs through xxl)
- Most common: 12.dp, 16.dp, 8.dp for padding/spacing

**Typography Usage:**
- `titleMedium` - Section headers
- `bodyMedium` - Main content
- `bodySmall` - Secondary text
- `labelSmall` - Badges, counts
- `headlineSmall` / `headlineLarge` - Major headings

**Design System Observations:**
- No custom Material Design overrides
- Clean, minimal top bar design (CompactTopBar at 48.dp)
- Heavy use of Material3 defaults
- Consistent color theming (light/dark modes fully implemented)
- Navigation via bottom bar + detail screens as modals/overlays

     1→# Hackers'Pub Android — Full UI Redesign
     2→
     3→**Date:** 2026-03-19
     4→**Status:** Approved
     5→**Goal:** Replace the default Material Design 3 styling with a custom, Ivory-inspired design system that gives Hackers'Pub a distinctive, editorial identity across both light and dark themes.
     6→
     7→## Design Philosophy
     8→
     9→Platform-agnostic custom identity — not Material, not iOS-native, but a unique Hackers'Pub look inspired by Ivory (Tapbots' Mastodon client). Near-monochrome palette with Deep Stone accent. Typography-first: font weight carries emphasis rather than color. Semantic colors (red, green) reserved for interactive state changes, giving them maximum impact against the neutral base.
    10→
    11→## Design System
    12→
    13→### Color Palette
    14→
    15→#### Light Theme
    16→| Token | Value | Usage |
    17→|-------|-------|-------|
    18→| `background` | `#FFFFFF` | Screen background |
    19→| `surface` | `#F2F2F7` | Dividers, secondary surfaces, icon button backgrounds |
    20→| `textPrimary` | `#1C1C1E` | Headlines, names, primary content |
    21→| `textBody` | `#3A3A3C` | Body text, post content |
    22→| `textSecondary` | `#AEAEB2` | Handles, timestamps, inactive tab labels |
    23→| `accent` | `#44403C` | Active tab icons/labels, links (via weight), follow buttons, interactive elements |
    24→| `accentMuted` | `#78716C` | Hashtags, relationship tags background at 10% opacity |
    25→| `divider` | `#F2F2F7` | Hairline separators between posts |
    26→| `buttonOutline` | `#D6D3D1` | Secondary button borders |
    27→| `error` / `reaction` | `#E8453C` | Heart reactions, error states, notification dots |
    28→| `share` | `#34D399` | Share/repost active state |
    29→
    30→#### Dark Theme
    31→| Token | Value | Usage |
    32→|-------|-------|-------|
    33→| `background` | `#171717` | Screen background |
    34→| `surface` | `#262626` | Dividers, secondary surfaces, icon button backgrounds |
    35→| `textPrimary` | `#FAFAFA` | Headlines, names |
    36→| `textBody` | `#D4D4D4` | Body text |
    37→| `textSecondary` | `#737373` | Handles, timestamps, inactive tabs |
    38→| `accent` | `#D6D3D1` | Active tab icons/labels, interactive elements (inverted stone) |
    39→| `accentMuted` | `#A8A29E` | Hashtags, tags |
    40→| `divider` | `#262626` | Hairline separators |
    41→| `buttonOutline` | `#525252` | Secondary button borders |
    42→| `error` / `reaction` | `#E8453C` | Same as light |
    43→| `share` | `#34D399` | Same as light |
    44→
    45→### Typography
    46→
    47→System default (Roboto on Android). No custom fonts — emphasis via weight hierarchy only.
    48→
    49→| Style | Size | Weight | Usage |
    50→|-------|------|--------|-------|
    51→| `titleLarge` | 22sp | 700 (Bold) | Screen titles (Timeline, Explore, etc.) |
    52→| `titleMedium` | 20sp | 700 (Bold) | Profile name |
    53→| `bodyLargeSemiBold` | 15sp | 600 (SemiBold) | Author names in post cards |
    54→| `bodyLarge` | 15sp | 400 (Regular) | Post content, bio text |
    55→| `bodyMedium` | 14sp | 400 (Regular) | Stats labels, settings items |
    56→| `labelMedium` | 13sp | 400 (Regular) | Handles, timestamps, engagement counts |
    57→| `labelSmall` | 12sp | 500 (Medium) | Hashtags, relationship tags |
    58→| `caption` | 11sp | 500 (Medium) | Tag pills, repost indicators |
    59→| `tabLabel` | 10sp | 600 (SemiBold) | Bottom tab labels |
    60→
    61→**Letter spacing:** -0.3sp on `titleLarge` for tighter headline feel. Default elsewhere.
    62→
    63→### Spacing
    64→
    65→Base unit: 4dp. Consistent throughout.
    66→
    67→| Token | Value | Usage |
    68→|-------|-------|-------|
    69→| `xs` | 4dp | Fine spacing (between name and handle) |
    70→| `sm` | 8dp | Compact spacing (engagement icon gaps) |
    71→| `md` | 12dp | Element spacing (avatar-to-text gap, between post sections) |
    72→| `lg` | 16dp | Standard padding (screen edges, post padding) |
    73→| `xl` | 24dp | Section spacing (stats row gaps) |
    74→| `xxl` | 32dp | Large section spacing (sign-in margins) |
    75→
    76→### Shapes
    77→
    78→| Element | Radius | Size |
    79→|---------|--------|------|
    80→| Avatar (timeline) | Circle | 42dp |
    81→| Avatar (profile) | Circle | 80dp |
    82→| Avatar (repost indicator) | Circle | 16dp |
    83→| Pill buttons (Follow, etc.) | 20dp | Height ~36dp |
    84→| Icon buttons (compose, etc.) | Circle | 28dp |
    85→| Relationship tags | Full capsule | Auto-width |
    86→| Notification dot | Circle | 8dp |
    87→
    88→No card elevation or shadows anywhere. Post separation via hairline dividers only.
    89→
    90→### Icons
    91→
    92→Outlined stroke icons (Feather/Lucide style), 22dp for tab bar, 16dp for engagement bar. Stroke width 1.5dp.
    93→
    94→- Active state: filled icon with accent color
    95→- Inactive state: outlined icon with secondary color
    96→- Engagement active states per action:
    97→  - Reply: `accent` color when active (user has replied)
    98→  - Share/Repost: `share` green (`#34D399`) when active
    99→  - Reaction/Heart: `reaction` red (`#E8453C`) when active, filled
   100→  - Quote: `accent` color when active (user has quoted)
   101→  - External share: no active state, always `textSecondary`
   102→
   103→### Bottom Navigation Bar
   104→
   105→- Height: 56dp (standard)
   106→- Background: `background` color (white in light, `#171717` in dark)
   107→- Top border: 1dp hairline in `divider` color
   108→- 4 tabs (authenticated): Home, Explore, Alerts, Search
   109→- 4 tabs (unauthenticated): Local, Fediverse, Search, Sign In
   110→- Icon size: 22dp, stroke width 1.5dp
   111→- Label: 10sp/600 (`tabLabel`), 3dp below icon
   112→- Active tab: filled icon + label in `accent` color
   113→- Inactive tab: outlined icon + label in `textSecondary` color
   114→- Notification dot: 8dp red circle (`reaction` color), positioned top-right of Alerts icon with 2dp white border
   115→
   116→### Media Grid
   117→
   118→- Images: 8dp border-radius on all corners
   119→- Single image: full width, max-height 300dp, `contentScale = Crop`
   120→- Two images: side-by-side, 4dp gap between
   121→- Three+ images: 2x2 grid, 4dp gaps
   122→- Container: no border, no elevation, clips to rounded shape
   123→
   124→## Screen Specifications
   125→
   126→### Timeline Screen
   127→
   128→**Header:**
   129→- Large bold title "Timeline" (22sp/700) top-left
   130→- Right side: compose button (28dp circle, surface background, + icon) and user avatar (28dp circle)
   131→- No border below header — content starts immediately
   132→
   133→**Content:**
   134→- Vertical scrolling list (LazyColumn)
   135→- Posts separated by 1dp hairline dividers (`divider` color) with 16dp horizontal margin
   136→- Pull-to-refresh support
   137→
   138→**Post Card Layout:**
   139→- 16dp horizontal padding, 12dp vertical padding
   140→- Repost indicator (if shared): 16dp avatar + repost icon + actor name, caption size, secondary color, indented to align with post content (54dp left padding = 42dp avatar + 12dp gap)
   141→- Author row: 42dp circular avatar, 12dp gap, then name (`bodyLargeSemiBold`, `textPrimary`) + timestamp (`labelMedium`, `textSecondary`) on same baseline
   142→- Body text: 15sp/400, `textBody` color, 4dp below author row
   143→- Hashtags: 11sp/500, `accentMuted` color, 6dp below body
   144→- Engagement bar: 10dp below content, icons (16dp) + counts (13sp), 18dp gaps between items. Muted by default, semantic colors when active (red heart filled, green repost)
   145→
   146→**Quoted Post (if present):**
   147→- Indented block below body text
   148→- 1dp border in `divider` color, 8dp border-radius, 8dp padding
   149→- Smaller avatar (32dp), author info, truncated content
   150→
   151→**Floating Action Button:** None — compose is in the header
   152→
   153→### Post Detail Screen
   154→
   155→**Header:** Back arrow (left) + ellipsis menu (right), title "Post" centered (`bodyLargeSemiBold`)
   156→
   157→**Post Content (expanded view):**
   158→- Same author row as timeline post card (avatar, name, handle)
   159→- Full body text rendering (no truncation), `bodyLarge`
   160→- Full timestamp below body: absolute date/time format, `labelMedium`, `textSecondary`
   161→- Visibility indicator: icon + label, `caption`, `textSecondary`
   162→
   163→**Article posts:** If post type is Article, show article title in `titleMedium` (20sp/700) above body content, with an "Article" badge (`caption`, `accentMuted` at 10% opacity background, capsule shape)
   164→
   165→**Engagement Stats Row:**
   166→- Horizontal row below post content, 12dp vertical padding
   167→- Counts displayed as: "12 Shares · 23 Reactions · 4 Quotes"
   168→- `bodyMedium`, counts in `textPrimary` (bold), labels in `textSecondary`
   169→- Tappable — opens bottom sheet with actor list
   170→
   171→**Action Bar:**
   172→- Below stats, separated by hairline dividers above and below
   173→- Full-width row with evenly spaced action icons: Reply, Share, React, Quote, Delete (owner only), External Share
   174→- Icons: 20dp, `textSecondary` by default, semantic colors when active
   175→- Delete icon: `reaction` red color
   176→
   177→**Reaction Groups:**
   178→- Below action bar if reactions exist
   179→- Horizontal scrollable row of emoji pills
   180→- Each pill: emoji + count, `surface` background, 16dp border-radius, 8dp horizontal padding
   181→- Active (user reacted): `accent` at 10% opacity background, `accent` text
   182→
   183→**Reaction Picker (bottom sheet):**
   184→- Modal bottom sheet, `background` color
   185→- Grid of available emoji reactions, 7 columns
   186→- Each cell: 40dp, 8dp border-radius
   187→- Active reactions highlighted with `accent` at 15% opacity background
   188→- Close button top-right
   189→
   190→**Shares/Quotes List (bottom sheet):**
   191→- Modal bottom sheet with navigation title ("Shares" or "Quotes")
   192→- List of actor rows: 40dp circular avatar, name (`bodyLargeSemiBold`), handle (`labelMedium`, `textSecondary`)
   193→- "Load more" button at bottom if paginated
   194→- Empty state: centered "No shares yet" / "No quotes yet"
   195→
   196→**Reply Thread:**
   197→- Below the main post, same post card format as timeline
   198→- Replies listed with hairline dividers
   199→- Reply FAB: 56dp circle, `accent` background, white reply icon, bottom-right positioned, 16dp margin from edges
   200→
   201→### Profile Screen
   202→
   203→**Header:** Back arrow (left) + ellipsis menu (right), no title text
   204→
   205→**Profile Section (centered):**
   206→- 80dp circular avatar
   207→- Name: 20sp/700, 12dp below avatar
   208→- Handle: 14sp/400, `textSecondary`, 2dp below name
   209→- Bio: 14sp/400, `textBody`, centered, 10dp below handle, 24dp horizontal margin
   210→- Stats row: 24dp gap between items, centered, 14dp below bio
   211→  - Each stat: count (16sp/700) above label (12sp/secondary)
   212→  - Three items: Posts, Followers, Following
   213→- Action buttons: 16dp below stats, centered
   214→  - Follow: pill button, `accent` background, white text, 28dp horizontal padding
   215→  - Share: pill button, transparent, `buttonOutline` border, share icon only
   216→- Relationship tag: "Follows you" capsule, `accentMuted` at 10% opacity background, 11sp/500, 10dp below buttons
   217→
   218→**Content:** Hairline divider, then post list identical to timeline (without avatars if showing own profile — keep avatars for consistency)
   219→
   220→### Notifications Screen
   221→
   222→**Header:** Large bold title "Notifications" (same as timeline)
   223→
   224→**Notification Rows:**
   225→- 42dp actor avatar (circular), 12dp gap
   226→- Action text: actor name (bold) + action description + timestamp
   227→- Notification type indicated by text ("liked your post", "shared your post", "followed you")
   228→- Notification type icons: all use `textSecondary` color (monochrome, consistent with design philosophy — no per-type color coding)
   229→- Hairline dividers between items
   230→- Unread indicator: red dot (8dp) on the Alerts tab icon
   231→- Empty state: centered text "No notifications yet"
   232→
   233→### Explore Screen
   234→
   235→**Header:** Large bold title "Explore"
   236→
   237→**Segmented Tabs:** Below header, "Local" / "Global" segmented control
   238→- Active tab: `accent` text, underline indicator
   239→- Inactive tab: `textSecondary`
   240→
   241→**Content:** Same post list as timeline, scoped to local or global feed
   242→
   243→### Search Screen
   244→
   245→**Header:** Large bold title "Search"
   246→
   247→**Search Bar:** Rounded text field below header, `surface` background, 12dp border-radius, search icon left-aligned, 16dp horizontal margin
   248→
   249→**Results:** Actor rows (avatar + name + handle) and post cards, depending on result type
   250→
   251→### Compose Screen
   252→
   253→**Header:** Navigation bar style
   254→- Left: "Cancel" text button (`accent` color)
   255→- Right: "Post" pill button (`accent` background, white text), disabled state at 40% opacity
   256→- Title: "Compose", "Reply", or "Quoting" centered (`bodyLargeSemiBold`, 15sp/600)
   257→
   258→**Content:**
   259→- Reply indicator (if replying): "Replying to @handle" in `textSecondary`, 13sp
   260→- Text editor: full-width, 15sp, placeholder "What's on your mind?"
   261→- Markdown support with live preview toggle
   262→
   263→**Bottom toolbar:** Visibility selector, language picker — subtle icons in `textSecondary`
   264→
   265→### Settings Screen
   266→
   267→**Header:** Large bold title "Settings"
   268→
   269→**Content:** Grouped list sections with section headers (13sp/500, uppercase, `textSecondary`)
   270→
   271→Sections:
   272→- **App Info:** Logo (80dp, 16dp corner radius), app name, version
   273→- **Typography:** Font size slider (0.75x–2.0x)
   274→- **Timeline:** Markdown truncation options
   275→- **Engagement:** Confirmation toggles
   276→- **Links:** In-app browser toggle
   277→- **About:** Open source licenses, links
   278→
   279→Each row: standard list item, 16dp padding, `textPrimary` label, `textSecondary` value/description. Hairline dividers between rows.
   280→
   281→### Sign-In Screen
   282→
   283→**Layout:** Centered vertically
   284→
   285→- Logo: 120dp, centered
   286→- Title: "Hackers' Pub" (22sp/700), 16dp below logo
   287→- Subtitle: "Sign in to continue" (14sp, `textSecondary`), 4dp below title
   288→- Username field: rounded border (12dp radius), `surface` background, 32dp below subtitle, 32dp horizontal margin
   289→- Verification code field: same style (appears after username submission)
   290→- Primary button: pill shape, `accent` background, white text, full width within margins
   291→- Error text: `error` color, 13sp, below button
   292→
   293→## Migration Strategy
   294→
   295→This redesign replaces the existing Material Design 3 theming while keeping the same Compose architecture. Changes are purely in the UI layer:
   296→
   297→1. **Theme.kt** — Replace M3 color scheme with custom color tokens
   298→2. **Spacing.kt** — Already exists, minor adjustments to values
   299→3. **New: Typography.kt** — Define custom text styles (not M3 Typography)
   300→4. **New: Shapes.kt** — Define shape tokens
   301→5. **New: Icons.kt** — Custom icon set (if switching from Material Icons to Feather/Lucide)
   302→6. **Each screen** — Replace M3 components (TopAppBar, FloatingActionButton, etc.) with custom composables
   303→7. **PostCard** — Restyle engagement bar, remove card elevation, update spacing
   304→8. **CompactTopBar** — Replace with new LargeTitle header composable
   305→
   306→No changes to ViewModels, repositories, data layer, or navigation graph. Pure visual transformation.
   307→
   308→**Note on profile stats:** The current data model (`Actor`) does not include follower/following/post counts. The stats row in the profile screen should only display stats that are available from the API. If counts are not available, omit the stats row entirely rather than showing zeros.
   309→
   310→**Note on font scaling:** The custom typography definitions serve as base values. The existing `LocalFontScale` mechanism continues to apply — all `sp` values are multiplied by the user's font scale preference (0.75x–2.0x).
   311→
   312→## Out of Scope
   313→
   314→- Custom animations/transitions (can be added later)
   315→- Custom font families (using system Roboto)
   316→- Tablet/landscape layouts
   317→- Widget design
   318→- Onboarding flow
   319→
```
