---

**APPROVED** -- with minor advisory notes below. The plan is well-structured, has correct file paths, proper task ordering, and comprehensive spec coverage. No blockers were found.

Here is the detailed assessment:

## 1. Spec Coverage

Every design spec requirement maps to a plan task. Confirmed coverage:

| Spec Section | Plan Task(s) |
|---|---|
| Color Palette (light/dark) | Task 1 |
| Typography | Task 2 |
| Shapes | Task 3 |
| Bottom Navigation Bar | Task 5 |
| Screen titles (LargeTitleHeader) | Task 4 |
| Post Card Layout | Task 6 |
| Timeline Screen | Task 8 |
| Post Detail Screen | Task 13 |
| Profile Screen | Task 12 |
| Notifications Screen | Task 9 |
| Explore Screen | Task 10 |
| Search Screen | Task 11 |
| Compose Screen | Task 14 |
| Settings Screen | Task 15 |
| Sign-In Screen | Task 16 |
| HtmlContent / MentionAutocomplete / ReactionPicker | Task 7 |
| MainActivity / Cleanup | Task 17 |

## 2. File Accuracy

All referenced files exist at the expected paths under `app/src/main/java/pub/hackers/android/`:

- `ui/theme/Theme.kt` -- exists
- `ui/theme/Spacing.kt` -- exists
- `ui/components/CompactTopBar.kt` -- exists (to be replaced/deleted)
- `ui/components/PostCard.kt` -- exists
- `ui/components/HtmlContent.kt` -- exists
- `ui/components/MentionAutocomplete.kt` -- exists
- `ui/components/ReactionPicker.kt` -- exists
- `ui/HackersPubApp.kt` -- exists
- `ui/screens/timeline/TimelineScreen.kt` -- exists
- `ui/screens/notifications/NotificationsScreen.kt` -- exists
- `ui/screens/explore/ExploreScreen.kt` -- exists
- `ui/screens/search/SearchScreen.kt` -- exists
- `ui/screens/settings/SettingsScreen.kt` -- exists
- `ui/screens/profile/ProfileScreen.kt` -- exists
- `ui/screens/postdetail/PostDetailScreen.kt` -- exists
- `ui/screens/compose/ComposeScreen.kt` -- exists
- `ui/screens/auth/SignInScreen.kt` -- exists
- `MainActivity.kt` -- exists

New files to create (`Colors.kt`, `AppTypography.kt`, `AppShapes.kt`, `LargeTitleHeader.kt`, `BottomNavBar.kt`) do not yet exist, as expected.

## 3. Task Ordering

The dependency chain is correct:

- Tasks 1-3 (design tokens) are independent of each other but must precede Tasks 4+, which consume them. The plan correctly places them first and in order.
- Tasks 4-5 (LargeTitleHeader, BottomNavBar) create the core components that screens depend on. These are correctly placed before the screen tasks.
- Task 6 (PostCard) and Task 7 (supporting components) restyle shared components before screen-level work, which is the right order since screens compose these.
- Tasks 8-16 (screens) are correctly noted as parallelizable -- each modifies a single independent screen file.
- Task 17 (cleanup) correctly comes last since CompactTopBar deletion requires all screens to have been migrated first.

## 4. Code Correctness

The Kotlin/Compose code snippets in the plan are syntactically correct:

- `AppColorScheme` data class with `@Immutable` annotation and `staticCompositionLocalOf` is idiomatic Compose.
- `AppTextStyles` follows the same pattern correctly.
- `AppShapes` object with `dp` constants is valid.
- `LargeTitleHeader` signature with `@Composable` `RowScope.() -> Unit` trailing content is correct.
- `BottomNavBar` and `BottomNavItem` data class structure are valid.
- Color hex values in the plan match the design spec exactly (I cross-checked all 22 color tokens).

## 5. Missing Steps -- Advisory Notes (Non-Blocking)

These are minor observations a developer should be aware of, but none are blockers:

**A. `error` token naming discrepancy.** The design spec uses `error / reaction` naming for `#E8453C`. The plan's `AppColorScheme` only defines `reaction` and omits a separate `error` alias. This is fine -- the plan consolidates them into one token. However, a developer might want to add `val error: Color = reaction` as a convenience alias since `MaterialTheme.colorScheme.error` is used in several places today (e.g., delete buttons, sign-out, blocked tags).

**B. Unauthenticated bottom nav tab mismatch.** The spec says unauthenticated tabs are "Local, Fediverse, Search, Sign In" (4 tabs). The current code has 3 unauthenticated tabs: Explore, Search, Settings. The plan (Task 5, Step 2) mentions "Unauthenticated: keep existing pattern but with new component" which is vague. The developer will need to decide whether to match the spec's 4-tab unauthenticated layout or keep the current 3-tab approach. This is a product decision, not a code issue.

**C. Settings navigation relocation.** The plan correctly identifies that Settings moves from bottom nav to a header gear icon (Task 5, Step 2 and Task 8, Step 1). However, it should be noted that the current `Screen.Settings` route and navigation composable in `HackersPubApp.kt` must remain intact -- only its bottom nav entry is removed. The route itself stays in the `NavHost`. The plan implies this correctly but does not state it explicitly.

**D. Missing components in "Modified Files" table.** The `LoadingIndicator.kt` file (containing `FullScreenLoading` and `LoadingItem`) and `ErrorMessage.kt` are not listed for restyling. These use `MaterialTheme` colors and `CircularProgressIndicator` which will inherit M3 color mapping automatically through the Theme.kt changes, so this is not a problem -- just worth noting.

**E. Profile avatar size discrepancy.** The current `ProfileHeader` uses 96dp for the avatar. The plan (Task 12, Step 2) correctly specifies changing it to 80dp per the spec. This is covered.

**F. `Spacing.kt` and `Radius` object.** The plan says "No changes needed (values already match spec)" for `Spacing.kt`. Confirmed correct -- the existing values (`xs=4dp`, `sm=8dp`, `md=12dp`, `lg=16dp`, `xl=24dp`, `xxl=32dp`) match the spec exactly. Note that `AppShapes.kt` introduces some shape radii that overlap with the existing `Radius` object in `Spacing.kt` (e.g., `Radius.lg = 8dp` vs `AppShapes.mediaRadius = 8dp`). The developer should consider whether to keep both or consolidate to avoid confusion.

## Summary

The plan is thorough, correctly structured, and implementable as written. All file paths are verified, spec requirements are fully covered, task dependencies are properly ordered, and code snippets are syntactically valid. The advisory notes above are quality-of-life observations -- none would block a developer from executing the plan successfully.

    36→| `ui/components/ReactionPicker.kt` | Update colors/sizing to use design tokens |
    37→| `ui/screens/timeline/TimelineScreen.kt` | Replace CompactTopBar + FAB with LargeTitleHeader |

    39→| `ui/screens/explore/ExploreScreen.kt` | New header, restyle tabs |
    40→| `ui/screens/search/SearchScreen.kt` | New header, restyle search bar and results |
    41→| `ui/screens/settings/SettingsScreen.kt` | New header, restyle list items |
    42→| `ui/screens/profile/ProfileScreen.kt` | Centered layout, new sizing, pill buttons |
    43→| `ui/screens/postdetail/PostDetailScreen.kt` | New header, restyle engagement stats/action bar/sheets |

    45→| `ui/screens/auth/SignInScreen.kt` | Restyle fields and buttons to spec |

    52→## Task 1: Design System — Colors
    53→
    54→**Files:**
    55→- Create: `ui/theme/Colors.kt`
    56→- Modify: `ui/theme/Theme.kt`
    57→
    58→- [ ] **Step 1: Create `Colors.kt` with light and dark color token objects**
    59→
    60→```kotlin
    61→package pub.hackers.android.ui.theme
    62→
    63→import androidx.compose.runtime.Immutable
    64→import androidx.compose.runtime.staticCompositionLocalOf
    65→import androidx.compose.ui.graphics.Color
    66→
    67→@Immutable
    68→data class AppColorScheme(
    69→    val background: Color,
    70→    val surface: Color,
    71→    val textPrimary: Color,
    72→    val textBody: Color,
    73→    val textSecondary: Color,
    74→    val accent: Color,
    75→    val accentMuted: Color,
    76→    val divider: Color,
    77→    val buttonOutline: Color,
    78→    val reaction: Color,
    79→    val share: Color,
    80→)
    81→
    82→val LightAppColors = AppColorScheme(
    83→    background = Color(0xFFFFFFFF),
    84→    surface = Color(0xFFF2F2F7),
    85→    textPrimary = Color(0xFF1C1C1E),
    86→    textBody = Color(0xFF3A3A3C),
    87→    textSecondary = Color(0xFFAEAEB2),
    88→    accent = Color(0xFF44403C),
    89→    accentMuted = Color(0xFF78716C),
    90→    divider = Color(0xFFF2F2F7),
    91→    buttonOutline = Color(0xFFD6D3D1),
    92→    reaction = Color(0xFFE8453C),
    93→    share = Color(0xFF34D399),
    94→)
    95→
    96→val DarkAppColors = AppColorScheme(
    97→    background = Color(0xFF171717),
    98→    surface = Color(0xFF262626),
    99→    textPrimary = Color(0xFFFAFAFA),
   100→    textBody = Color(0xFFD4D4D4),
   101→    textSecondary = Color(0xFF737373),
   102→    accent = Color(0xFFD6D3D1),
   103→    accentMuted = Color(0xFFA8A29E),
   104→    divider = Color(0xFF262626),
   105→    buttonOutline = Color(0xFF525252),
   106→    reaction = Color(0xFFE8453C),
   107→    share = Color(0xFF34D399),
   108→)
   109→
   110→val LocalAppColors = staticCompositionLocalOf { LightAppColors }
   111→```
   112→
   113→- [ ] **Step 2: Update `Theme.kt` to provide `LocalAppColors`**
   114→
   115→Keep the existing `MaterialTheme` wrapper (needed for M3 components that still exist like `ModalBottomSheet`, `Switch`, `Slider`) but add `CompositionLocalProvider` for `LocalAppColors`. Update the M3 `colorScheme` to map to the new tokens so M3 components pick up our colors automatically.
   116→
   117→Key changes in `HackersPubTheme`:
   118→- Select `LightAppColors` or `DarkAppColors` based on `isSystemInDarkTheme()`
   119→- Map them into a `lightColorScheme()` / `darkColorScheme()` so M3 components still function
   120→- Wrap `MaterialTheme` content in `CompositionLocalProvider(LocalAppColors provides colors)`
   121→- Update status bar color to use `colors.background`
   122→
   123→- [ ] **Step 3: Verify the app builds and runs with no visual regression**
   124→
   125→Run: `./gradlew assembleDebug`
   126→
   127→At this point colors are provided but nothing consumes them yet — the app should look identical.
   128→
   129→- [ ] **Step 4: Commit**
   130→
   131→```
   132→git add -A && git commit -m "feat: add custom color token system (AppColorScheme)"
   133→```

   137→## Task 2: Design System — Typography
   138→
   139→**Files:**
   140→- Create: `ui/theme/AppTypography.kt`
   141→- Modify: `ui/theme/Theme.kt`
   142→
   143→- [ ] **Step 1: Create `AppTypography.kt` with all text styles**
   144→
   145→```kotlin
   146→package pub.hackers.android.ui.theme
   147→
   148→import androidx.compose.runtime.Immutable
   149→import androidx.compose.runtime.staticCompositionLocalOf
   150→import androidx.compose.ui.text.TextStyle
   151→import androidx.compose.ui.text.font.FontWeight
   152→import androidx.compose.ui.unit.sp
   153→
   154→@Immutable
   155→data class AppTextStyles(
   156→    val titleLarge: TextStyle,
   157→    val titleMedium: TextStyle,
   158→    val bodyLargeSemiBold: TextStyle,
   159→    val bodyLarge: TextStyle,
   160→    val bodyMedium: TextStyle,
   161→    val labelMedium: TextStyle,
   162→    val labelSmall: TextStyle,
   163→    val caption: TextStyle,
   164→    val tabLabel: TextStyle,
   165→)
   166→
   167→val AppTypographyDefaults = AppTextStyles(
   168→    titleLarge = TextStyle(fontSize = 22.sp, fontWeight = FontWeight.Bold, letterSpacing = (-0.3).sp),
   169→    titleMedium = TextStyle(fontSize = 20.sp, fontWeight = FontWeight.Bold),
   170→    bodyLargeSemiBold = TextStyle(fontSize = 15.sp, fontWeight = FontWeight.SemiBold),
   171→    bodyLarge = TextStyle(fontSize = 15.sp, fontWeight = FontWeight.Normal),
   172→    bodyMedium = TextStyle(fontSize = 14.sp, fontWeight = FontWeight.Normal),
   173→    labelMedium = TextStyle(fontSize = 13.sp, fontWeight = FontWeight.Normal),
   174→    labelSmall = TextStyle(fontSize = 12.sp, fontWeight = FontWeight.Medium),
   175→    caption = TextStyle(fontSize = 11.sp, fontWeight = FontWeight.Medium),
   176→    tabLabel = TextStyle(fontSize = 10.sp, fontWeight = FontWeight.SemiBold),
   177→)
   178→
   179→val LocalAppTypography = staticCompositionLocalOf { AppTypographyDefaults }
   180→```
   181→
   182→- [ ] **Step 2: Provide `LocalAppTypography` in `HackersPubTheme`**
   183→
   184→Add `LocalAppTypography provides AppTypographyDefaults` to the existing `CompositionLocalProvider` block. Ensure the existing `LocalFontScale` mechanism is preserved — font scaling is applied at the composable level, not in the type definitions.
   185→
   186→- [ ] **Step 3: Verify build**
   187→
   188→Run: `./gradlew assembleDebug`
   189→
   190→- [ ] **Step 4: Commit**
   191→
   192→```
   193→git add -A && git commit -m "feat: add custom typography token system (AppTextStyles)"
   194→```

   258→- [ ] **Step 1: Create `LargeTitleHeader.kt`**
   259→
   260→A composable that renders:
   261→- Large bold title (22sp/700) on the left
   262→- Optional trailing content (icons, avatar) on the right
   263→- No bottom border
   264→- Background: `AppColors.background`
   265→- Padding: 12dp vertical, 16dp horizontal
   266→
   267→```kotlin
   268→@Composable
   269→fun LargeTitleHeader(
   270→    title: String,
   271→    modifier: Modifier = Modifier,
   272→    trailingContent: @Composable (RowScope.() -> Unit)? = null,
   273→)
   274→```
   275→
   276→Implementation:
   277→- `Row` with `verticalAlignment = CenterVertically`
   278→- `Text` with `LocalAppTypography.current.titleLarge` and `LocalAppColors.current.textPrimary`
   279→- `Spacer(Modifier.weight(1f))` between title and trailing
   280→- Trailing content in a `Row` with `horizontalArrangement = spacedBy(16.dp)`

   294→## Task 5: Core Component — BottomNavBar

   300→- [ ] **Step 1: Create `BottomNavBar.kt`**
   301→
   302→Custom bottom navigation bar matching spec:
   303→- Height: 56dp
   304→- Background: `AppColors.background`
   305→- Top border: 1dp `AppColors.divider`
   306→- Each tab: icon (22dp) + label (tabLabel style), 3dp gap

   308→- Support for notification dot overlay on Alerts tab
   309→
   310→```kotlin
   311→@Composable
   312→fun BottomNavBar(
   313→    items: List<BottomNavItem>,
   314→    selectedRoute: String,
   315→    onItemSelected: (BottomNavItem) -> Unit,
   316→    modifier: Modifier = Modifier,
   317→)
   318→
   319→data class BottomNavItem(
   320→    val route: String,
   321→    val label: String,
   322→    val icon: ImageVector,
   323→    val selectedIcon: ImageVector,
   324→    val hasNotificationDot: Boolean = false,
   325→)
   326→```
   327→

   329→
   330→Replace the M3 `NavigationBar` + `NavigationBarItem` block with `BottomNavBar`. Update tab configuration:
   331→- Authenticated: Home, Explore, Alerts, Search (4 tabs, Settings removed from bottom nav — accessible from header or profile)
   332→- Unauthenticated: keep existing pattern but with new component
   333→
   334→Key changes:

   336→- Define `BottomNavItem` list based on auth state
   337→- Replace the `bottomBar` lambda in `Scaffold` with `BottomNavBar`
   338→- Settings tab moves to a gear icon in the Timeline header trailing content
   339→
   340→**Important:** The current app has 5 tabs (Timeline, Notifications, Explore, Search, Settings) when authenticated. The spec calls for 4 tabs (Home, Explore, Alerts, Search). Settings needs to be accessible via a gear icon in the header or from the profile screen. Add a settings gear icon to the Timeline header's trailing content.

     1→# Code Quality Reviewer Prompt Template
     2→
     3→Use this template when dispatching a code quality reviewer subagent.
     4→
     5→**Purpose:** Verify implementation is well-built (clean, tested, maintainable)
     6→
     7→**Only dispatch after spec compliance review passes.**
     8→
     9→```
    10→Task tool (superpowers:code-reviewer):
    11→  Use template at requesting-code-review/code-reviewer.md
    12→
    13→  WHAT_WAS_IMPLEMENTED: [from implementer's report]
    14→  PLAN_OR_REQUIREMENTS: Task N from [plan-file]
    15→  BASE_SHA: [commit before task]
    16→  HEAD_SHA: [current commit]
    17→  DESCRIPTION: [task summary]
    18→```
    19→
    20→**In addition to standard code quality concerns, the reviewer should check:**
    21→- Does each file have one clear responsibility with a well-defined interface?
    22→- Are units decomposed so they can be understood and tested independently?
    23→- Is the implementation following the file structure from the plan?
    24→- Did this implementation create new files that are already large, or significantly grow existing files? (Don't flag pre-existing file sizes — focus on what this change contributed.)
    25→
    26→**Code reviewer returns:** Strengths, Issues (Critical/Important/Minor), Assessment
    27→
```

```
     1→# Implementer Subagent Prompt Template
     2→
     3→Use this template when dispatching an implementer subagent.
     4→
     5→```
     6→Task tool (general-purpose):
     7→  description: "Implement Task N: [task name]"
     8→  prompt: |
     9→    You are implementing Task N: [task name]
    10→
    11→    ## Task Description
    12→
    13→    [FULL TEXT of task from plan - paste it here, don't make subagent read file]
    14→
    15→    ## Context
    16→
    17→    [Scene-setting: where this fits, dependencies, architectural context]
    18→
    19→    ## Before You Begin
    20→
    21→    If you have questions about:
    22→    - The requirements or acceptance criteria
    23→    - The approach or implementation strategy
    24→    - Dependencies or assumptions
    25→    - Anything unclear in the task description
    26→
    27→    **Ask them now.** Raise any concerns before starting work.
    28→
    29→    ## Your Job
    30→
    31→    Once you're clear on requirements:
    32→    1. Implement exactly what the task specifies
    33→    2. Write tests (following TDD if task says to)
    34→    3. Verify implementation works
    35→    4. Commit your work
    36→    5. Self-review (see below)
    37→    6. Report back
    38→
    39→    Work from: [directory]
    40→
    41→    **While you work:** If you encounter something unexpected or unclear, **ask questions**.
    42→    It's always OK to pause and clarify. Don't guess or make assumptions.
    43→
    44→    ## Code Organization
    45→
    46→    You reason best about code you can hold in context at once, and your edits are more
    47→    reliable when files are focused. Keep this in mind:
    48→    - Follow the file structure defined in the plan
    49→    - Each file should have one clear responsibility with a well-defined interface
    50→    - If a file you're creating is growing beyond the plan's intent, stop and report
    51→      it as DONE_WITH_CONCERNS — don't split files on your own without plan guidance
    52→    - If an existing file you're modifying is already large or tangled, work carefully
    53→      and note it as a concern in your report
    54→    - In existing codebases, follow established patterns. Improve code you're touching
    55→      the way a good developer would, but don't restructure things outside your task.
    56→
    57→    ## When You're in Over Your Head
    58→
    59→    It is always OK to stop and say "this is too hard for me." Bad work is worse than
    60→    no work. You will not be penalized for escalating.
    61→
    62→    **STOP and escalate when:**
    63→    - The task requires architectural decisions with multiple valid approaches
    64→    - You need to understand code beyond what was provided and can't find clarity
    65→    - You feel uncertain about whether your approach is correct
    66→    - The task involves restructuring existing code in ways the plan didn't anticipate
    67→    - You've been reading file after file trying to understand the system without progress
    68→
    69→    **How to escalate:** Report back with status BLOCKED or NEEDS_CONTEXT. Describe
    70→    specifically what you're stuck on, what you've tried, and what kind of help you need.
    71→    The controller can provide more context, re-dispatch with a more capable model,
    72→    or break the task into smaller pieces.
    73→
    74→    ## Before Reporting Back: Self-Review
    75→
    76→    Review your work with fresh eyes. Ask yourself:
    77→
    78→    **Completeness:**
    79→    - Did I fully implement everything in the spec?
    80→    - Did I miss any requirements?
    81→    - Are there edge cases I didn't handle?
    82→
    83→    **Quality:**
    84→    - Is this my best work?
    85→    - Are names clear and accurate (match what things do, not how they work)?
    86→    - Is the code clean and maintainable?
    87→
    88→    **Discipline:**
    89→    - Did I avoid overbuilding (YAGNI)?
    90→    - Did I only build what was requested?
    91→    - Did I follow existing patterns in the codebase?
    92→
    93→    **Testing:**
    94→    - Do tests actually verify behavior (not just mock behavior)?
    95→    - Did I follow TDD if required?
    96→    - Are tests comprehensive?
    97→
    98→    If you find issues during self-review, fix them now before reporting.
    99→
   100→    ## Report Format
   101→
   102→    When done, report:
   103→    - **Status:** DONE | DONE_WITH_CONCERNS | BLOCKED | NEEDS_CONTEXT
   104→    - What you implemented (or what you attempted, if blocked)
   105→    - What you tested and test results
   106→    - Files changed
   107→    - Self-review findings (if any)
   108→    - Any issues or concerns
   109→
   110→    Use DONE_WITH_CONCERNS if you completed the work but have doubts about correctness.
   111→    Use BLOCKED if you cannot complete the task. Use NEEDS_CONTEXT if you need
   112→    information that wasn't provided. Never silently produce work you're unsure about.
   113→```
   114→
```

     1→# Spec Compliance Reviewer Prompt Template
     2→
     3→Use this template when dispatching a spec compliance reviewer subagent.
     4→
     5→**Purpose:** Verify implementer built what was requested (nothing more, nothing less)
     6→
     7→```
     8→Task tool (general-purpose):
     9→  description: "Review spec compliance for Task N"
    10→  prompt: |
    11→    You are reviewing whether an implementation matches its specification.
    12→
    13→    ## What Was Requested
    14→
    15→    [FULL TEXT of task requirements]
    16→
    17→    ## What Implementer Claims They Built
    18→
    19→    [From implementer's report]
    20→
    21→    ## CRITICAL: Do Not Trust the Report
    22→
    23→    The implementer finished suspiciously quickly. Their report may be incomplete,
    24→    inaccurate, or optimistic. You MUST verify everything independently.
    25→
    26→    **DO NOT:**
    27→    - Take their word for what they implemented
    28→    - Trust their claims about completeness
    29→    - Accept their interpretation of requirements
    30→
    31→    **DO:**
    32→    - Read the actual code they wrote
    33→    - Compare actual implementation to requirements line by line
    34→    - Check for missing pieces they claimed to implement
    35→    - Look for extra features they didn't mention
    36→
    37→    ## Your Job
    38→
    39→    Read the implementation code and verify:
    40→
    41→    **Missing requirements:**
    42→    - Did they implement everything that was requested?
    43→    - Are there requirements they skipped or missed?
    44→    - Did they claim something works but didn't actually implement it?
    45→
    46→    **Extra/unneeded work:**
    47→    - Did they build things that weren't requested?
    48→    - Did they over-engineer or add unnecessary features?
    49→    - Did they add "nice to haves" that weren't in spec?
    50→
    51→    **Misunderstandings:**
    52→    - Did they interpret requirements differently than intended?
    53→    - Did they solve the wrong problem?
    54→    - Did they implement the right feature but wrong way?
    55→
    56→    **Verify by reading code, not by trusting report.**
    57→
    58→    Report:
    59→    - ✅ Spec compliant (if everything matches after code inspection)
    60→    - ❌ Issues found: [list specifically what's missing or extra, with file:line references]
    61→```
    62→
```

All done. Here is a summary of what was changed in `/Users/kodingwarrior/fediverse/hackerspub-android/app/src/main/java/pub/hackers/android/ui/components/PostCard.kt`:

**Step 1 - Container:** Replaced `Card` with a plain `Column` using 16dp horizontal and 12dp vertical padding. Removed `CardDefaults.cardColors` and `CardDefaults.cardElevation`.

**Step 2 - Avatar and author row:** Avatar changed from 48dp to `AppShapes.avatarTimeline` (42dp). Name uses `LocalAppTypography.current.bodyLargeSemiBold` + `LocalAppColors.current.textPrimary`. Handle removed from inline display. Timestamp uses `LocalAppTypography.current.labelMedium` + `LocalAppColors.current.textSecondary` with a "· " prefix.

**Step 3 - Body text:** Content area now flows under the author row inside the name column (alongside avatar). HtmlContent inherits theme colors already via `MaterialTheme.colorScheme.onSurface`.

**Step 4 - EngagementBar:** Icons reduced to 16dp. Items spaced 18dp apart using `Arrangement.spacedBy(18.dp)`. Each engagement type has its own active color: Reply uses `accent`, Share uses `share`, React uses `reaction` (with filled icon when active), Quote uses `accent`. External share always uses `textSecondary`. Counts use `labelMedium` typography. Added `activeColor` parameter to `EngagementButton`.

**Step 5 - QuotedPostPreview:** Replaced `Surface` with a `Column` using `border()` modifier (1dp `divider` color, 8dp radius via `AppShapes.quotedPostRadius`). Transparent background. Avatar increased to `AppShapes.avatarQuoted` (32dp). Text colors use `textPrimary` for name and `textSecondary` for handle/timestamp.

**Step 6 - MediaGrid:** All `RoundedCornerShape(12.dp)` changed to `RoundedCornerShape(AppShapes.mediaRadius)` (8dp).

**Step 7 - Repost indicator:** Added 54dp start padding (42dp avatar + 12dp gap). Replaced the `Icon` with an `AsyncImage` showing the reposter's avatar at `AppShapes.avatarRepost` (16dp). Text uses `LocalAppTypography.current.caption` + `LocalAppColors.current.textSecondary`.

Build verified successfully with `./gradlew assembleDebug`. Committed as `b5c5911`.

The full UI redesign is complete. Here's the summary:

**22 files changed** across 8 commits:

**Design System (new):**
- `Colors.kt` — Custom color tokens (Deep Stone `#44403C` accent, light/dark)
- `AppTypography.kt` — 9 text styles with weight-based hierarchy
- `AppShapes.kt` — Standardized dimensions for avatars, pills, radii
- `LargeTitleHeader.kt` — Ivory-style large bold title header
- `BottomNavBar.kt` — Custom 4-tab bottom nav with notification dot

**Components restyled:**
- `PostCard.kt` — Removed card elevation, hairline dividers, 42dp circular avatars, new engagement bar colors
- `HtmlContent.kt` — Stone accent links instead of blue
- `MentionAutocomplete.kt` / `ReactionPicker.kt` — Design token colors

**All 9 screens restyled:**
- Timeline, Notifications, Explore, Search, Profile, Post Detail, Compose, Settings, Sign-In

**Removed:** `CompactTopBar.kt` (replaced by LargeTitleHeader)

Build passes cleanly. The app now has a distinctive Ivory-inspired editorial identity with the Deep Stone near-monochrome palette.

   259→                    val paddingPx = with(density) { 16.dp.toPx() }
   260→                    val cursorYInBox = cursorRect.bottom - scrollState.value + paddingPx
   261→                    val cursorXInBox = cursorRect.left + paddingPx
   262→

   264→                    val visibleCursorY = cursorYInBox.coerceIn(paddingPx, textFieldBounds.height - paddingPx)
   265→

   267→                        alignment = Alignment.TopStart,
   268→                        offset = IntOffset(
   269→                            x = cursorXInBox.roundToInt().coerceIn(0, (textFieldBounds.width - with(density) { 280.dp.toPx() }).toInt().coerceAtLeast(0)),
   270→                            y = (visibleCursorY + with(density) { 20.dp.toPx() }).roundToInt()
   271→                        ),

    41→    val error: String? = null,
    42→    // Mention autocomplete state
    43→    val mentionQuery: String? = null,
    44→    val mentionStartIndex: Int = -1,
    45→    val mentionSuggestions: List<Actor> = emptyList(),
    46→    val isLoadingMentions: Boolean = false
    47→)
    48→
    49→@OptIn(FlowPreview::class)
    50→@HiltViewModel
    51→class ComposeViewModel @Inject constructor(
    52→    private val repository: HackersPubRepository,
    53→    @ApplicationContext private val context: Context
    54→) : ViewModel() {
    55→
    56→    private val _uiState = MutableStateFlow(ComposeUiState())
    57→    val uiState: StateFlow<ComposeUiState> = _uiState.asStateFlow()
    58→
    59→    private var viewerHandle: String? = null
    60→
    61→    // Regex to detect @mention at cursor position
    62→    // Matches: @handle or @user@domain patterns that are incomplete
    63→    private val mentionRegex = Regex("""@([^\s@]+(?:@[^\s@]*)?)$""")
    64→
    65→    private val mentionQueryFlow = MutableSharedFlow<String>(replay = 0)
    66→
    67→    init {
    68→        viewModelScope.launch {
    69→            repository.getViewer().onSuccess { viewer ->
    70→                viewerHandle = viewer?.handle
    71→            }
    72→        }
    73→
    74→        // Setup debounced mention search
    75→        viewModelScope.launch {
    76→            mentionQueryFlow
    77→                .debounce(300L)
    78→                .collectLatest { query ->
    79→                    if (query.isNotEmpty()) {
    80→                        searchMentions(query)
    81→                    }
    82→                }
    83→        }
    84→    }
    85→
    86→    private suspend fun searchMentions(query: String) {
    87→        _uiState.update { it.copy(isLoadingMentions = true) }
    88→
    89→        repository.searchActorsByHandle(query, limit = 10)
    90→            .onSuccess { actors ->
    91→                _uiState.update {
    92→                    it.copy(
    93→                        mentionSuggestions = actors,
    94→                        isLoadingMentions = false
    95→                    )
    96→                }
    97→            }
    98→            .onFailure {
    99→                _uiState.update {
   100→                    it.copy(
   101→                        mentionSuggestions = emptyList(),
   102→                        isLoadingMentions = false
   103→                    )
   104→                }
   105→            }
   106→    }
   107→
   108→    private fun detectMentionTrigger(content: String, cursorPosition: Int) {
   109→        // Get text before cursor
   110→        val textBeforeCursor = content.take(cursorPosition)
   111→
   112→        // Find mention pattern at cursor position
   113→        val match = mentionRegex.find(textBeforeCursor)
   114→
   115→        if (match != null) {
   116→            val mentionQuery = match.groupValues[1]
   117→            val mentionStartIndex = match.range.first
   118→
   119→            _uiState.update {
   120→                it.copy(
   121→                    mentionQuery = mentionQuery,
   122→                    mentionStartIndex = mentionStartIndex
   123→                )
   124→            }
   125→
   126→            // Trigger debounced search
   127→            viewModelScope.launch {
   128→                mentionQueryFlow.emit(mentionQuery)
   129→            }
   130→        } else {
   131→            // Clear mention state
   132→            clearMentionState()
   133→        }
   134→    }
   135→
   136→    private fun clearMentionState() {
   137→        _uiState.update {
   138→            it.copy(
   139→                mentionQuery = null,
   140→                mentionStartIndex = -1,
   141→                mentionSuggestions = emptyList(),
   142→                isLoadingMentions = false
   143→            )
   144→        }
   145→    }
   146→
   147→    fun selectMention(actor: Actor): Pair<String, Int> {
   148→        val state = _uiState.value
   149→        val content = state.content
   150→        val startIndex = state.mentionStartIndex
   151→
   152→        if (startIndex >= 0) {
   153→            // Replace @partial with @full_handle
   154→            val beforeMention = content.take(startIndex)
   155→            val textAfterMentionStart = content.drop(startIndex)
   156→
   157→            // Find where the partial mention ends (next space, newline, or end of string)
   158→            val mentionEndOffset = textAfterMentionStart.indexOfFirst { it == ' ' || it == '\n' }
   159→            val afterMention = if (mentionEndOffset >= 0) {
   160→                textAfterMentionStart.drop(mentionEndOffset)
   161→            } else {
   162→                " " // Add space after mention if at end
   163→            }
   164→
   165→            val insertedHandle = "@${actor.handle} "
   166→            val newContent = "$beforeMention$insertedHandle${afterMention.trimStart()}"
   167→            val newCursorPosition = beforeMention.length + insertedHandle.length
   168→
   169→            _uiState.update {
   170→                it.copy(
   171→                    content = newContent,
   172→                    cursorPosition = newCursorPosition,
   173→                    mentionQuery = null,
   174→                    mentionStartIndex = -1,
   175→                    mentionSuggestions = emptyList(),
   176→                    isLoadingMentions = false
   177→                )
   178→            }
   179→
   180→            return Pair(newContent, newCursorPosition)
   181→        }
   182→
   183→        return Pair(content, state.cursorPosition)
   184→    }
   185→
   186→    fun dismissMentionSuggestions() {
   187→        clearMentionState()
   188→    }
   189→
   190→    fun setQuotedPost(postId: String) {
   191→        _uiState.update { it.copy(quotedPostId = postId, isLoadingQuotedPost = true, quotedPostLoadFailed = false) }
   192→        viewModelScope.launch {
   193→            repository.getPostDetail(postId)
   194→                .onSuccess { result ->
   195→                    _uiState.update {
   196→                        it.copy(
   197→                            quotedPost = result.post,
   198→                            isLoadingQuotedPost = false
   199→                        )
   200→                    }
   201→                }
   202→                .onFailure {
   203→                    _uiState.update { it.copy(isLoadingQuotedPost = false, quotedPostLoadFailed = true) }
   204→                }
   205→        }
   206→    }
   207→
   208→    fun setReplyTarget(postId: String) {
   209→        _uiState.update { it.copy(replyToId = postId, isLoadingReplyTarget = true) }
   210→        viewModelScope.launch {
   211→            repository.getPostDetail(postId)
   212→                .onSuccess { result ->
   213→                    val post = result.post
   214→                    val mentionPrefix = buildMentionPrefix(post)
   215→                    _uiState.update {
   216→                        it.copy(
   217→                            replyTargetPost = post,
   218→                            isLoadingReplyTarget = false,
   219→                            content = mentionPrefix
   220→                        )
   221→                    }
   222→                }
   223→                .onFailure {
   224→                    _uiState.update { it.copy(isLoadingReplyTarget = false) }
   225→                }
   226→        }
   227→    }
   228→
   229→    private fun buildMentionPrefix(post: Post): String {
   230→        val mentions = mutableSetOf<String>()
   231→
   232→        // Add the post author
   233→        mentions.add(post.actor.handle)
   234→
   235→        // Add existing mentions from the post
   236→        mentions.addAll(post.mentions)
   237→
   238→        // Remove viewer's own handle if present
   239→        viewerHandle?.let { mentions.remove(it) }
   240→
   241→        return if (mentions.isNotEmpty()) {
   242→            mentions.joinToString(" ") { "@$it" } + " "
   243→        } else {
   244→            ""
   245→        }
   246→    }
   247→
   248→    fun updateContent(content: String, cursorPosition: Int = content.length) {
   249→        _uiState.update { it.copy(content = content, cursorPosition = cursorPosition) }
   250→
   251→        // Check for mention trigger
   252→        detectMentionTrigger(content, cursorPosition)
   253→
   254→        // Detect language
   255→        detectLanguage(content)
   256→    }
   257→
   258→    private fun detectLanguage(text: String) {
   259→        if (text.isBlank() || text.length < 20) return
   260→
   261→        try {
   262→            if (Build.VERSION.SDK_INT >= Build.VERSION_CODES.Q) {
   263→                val tcm = context.getSystemService(Context.TEXT_CLASSIFICATION_SERVICE) as? TextClassificationManager
   264→                val classifier = tcm?.textClassifier ?: return
   265→                val request = TextLanguage.Request.Builder(text).build()
   266→                val result = classifier.detectLanguage(request)
   267→                if (result.localeHypothesisCount > 0) {
   268→                    val topLocale = result.getLocale(0)
   269→                    val lang = topLocale.language
   270→                    if (lang.isNotEmpty()) {
   271→                        _uiState.update { it.copy(language = lang) }
   272→                    }
   273→                }
   274→            }
   275→        } catch (_: Exception) {
   276→            // Fallback: keep current language
   277→        }
   278→    }
   279→
   280→    fun updateVisibility(visibility: PostVisibility) {
   281→        _uiState.update { it.copy(visibility = visibility) }
   282→    }
   283→
   284→    fun post() {
   285→        val state = _uiState.value
   286→        if (state.content.isBlank() || state.isPosting) return
   287→
   288→        viewModelScope.launch {
   289→            _uiState.update { it.copy(isPosting = true, error = null) }
   290→
   291→            repository.createNote(
   292→                content = state.content,
   293→                language = state.language,
   294→                visibility = state.visibility,
   295→                replyTargetId = state.replyToId,
   296→                quotedPostId = state.quotedPostId
   297→            )
   298→                .onSuccess {
   299→                    _uiState.update { it.copy(isPosting = false, isPosted = true) }
   300→                }
   301→                .onFailure { error ->
   302→                    _uiState.update {
   303→                        it.copy(
   304→                            error = error.message,
   305→                            isPosting = false
   306→                        )
   307→                    }
   308→                }
   309→        }
   310→    }
   311→
   312→    fun clearError() {
   313→        _uiState.update { it.copy(error = null) }
   314→    }
   315→}
   316→
```

> AGENT

Found it. The iOS code shows `handle.hasPrefix("@") ? handle : "@\(handle)"` — meaning the API sometimes returns handles with a leading `@`. Both `buildMentionPrefix` and `selectMention` blindly prepend `@`, causing `@@handle`.

> TOOL

  "new_string": "            val normalizedHandle = actor.handle.removePrefix(\"@\")\n            val insertedHandle = \"@$normalizedHandle \""

> AGENT

Fixed. The issue was that the API returns handles that may already have a leading `@` (e.g., `@hongminhee@hackers.pub`), and the code was blindly prepending another `@`. Now all handles are normalized with `removePrefix("@")` before adding the `@` prefix in both `buildMentionPrefix` (for reply mentions) and `selectMention` (for autocomplete).

   283→// Step 4: Restyled EngagementBar
   284→@Composable
   285→private fun EngagementBar(
   286→    post: Post,
   287→    onReplyClick: (() -> Unit)?,
   288→    onShareClick: (() -> Unit)?,

   292→) {

   296→    val isReacted = post.reactionGroups.any { it.viewerHasReacted }
   297→
   298→    Row(

   300→        horizontalArrangement = Arrangement.spacedBy(18.dp)

   302→        // Reply

   307→            onClick = onReplyClick,
   308→            activeColor = colors.accent,
   309→            isActive = isReplied
   310→        )
   311→
   312→        // Share/Repost
   313→        EngagementButton(
   314→            icon = if (isShared) Icons.Filled.Repeat else Icons.Outlined.Repeat,
   315→            count = post.engagementStats.shares,
   316→            contentDescription = stringResource(R.string.shares),
   317→            onClick = onShareClick,
   318→            activeColor = colors.share,
   319→            isActive = isShared
   320→        )
   321→
   322→        // Heart/React

   325→            count = post.engagementStats.reactions,
   326→            contentDescription = stringResource(R.string.reactions),
   327→            onClick = onReactionClick,
   328→            activeColor = colors.reaction,
   329→            isActive = isReacted
   330→        )
   331→
   332→        // Quote
   333→        EngagementButton(
   334→            icon = Icons.Outlined.FormatQuote,
   335→            count = post.engagementStats.quotes,
   336→            contentDescription = stringResource(R.string.quotes),

   350→                    contentDescription = stringResource(R.string.share),
   351→                    tint = colors.textSecondary,
   352→                    modifier = Modifier.size(16.dp)
   353→                )

   358→
   359→@Composable
   360→private fun EngagementButton(
   361→    icon: androidx.compose.ui.graphics.vector.ImageVector,
   362→    count: Int,
   363→    contentDescription: String,
   364→    onClick: (() -> Unit)?,
   365→    isActive: Boolean = false,
   366→    activeColor: Color = LocalAppColors.current.accent
   367→) {
   368→    val colors = LocalAppColors.current
   369→    val typography = LocalAppTypography.current
   370→    val tint = if (isActive) activeColor else colors.textSecondary
   371→
   372→    Row(
   373→        verticalAlignment = Alignment.CenterVertically
   374→    ) {
   375→        IconButton(
   376→            onClick = { onClick?.invoke() },
   377→            enabled = onClick != null,
   378→            modifier = Modifier.size(32.dp)
   379→        ) {
   380→            Icon(
   381→                imageVector = icon,
   382→                contentDescription = contentDescription,
   383→                tint = tint,
   384→                modifier = Modifier.size(16.dp)
   385→            )
   386→        }
   387→        if (count > 0) {
   388→            Text(
   389→                text = formatCount(count),
   390→                style = typography.labelMedium,
   391→                color = tint
   392→            )
   393→        }
   394→    }
   395→}
   396→
   397→// Step 5: Restyled QuotedPostPreview
   398→@Composable
   399→fun QuotedPostPreview(
   400→    post: Post,
   401→    onClick: () -> Unit,
   402→    onProfileClick: (String) -> Unit
   403→) {
   404→    val colors = LocalAppColors.current
   405→    val typography = LocalAppTypography.current
   406→
   407→    Column(
   408→        modifier = Modifier
   409→            .fillMaxWidth()
   410→            .border(
   411→                width = 1.dp,
   412→                color = colors.divider,
   413→                shape = RoundedCornerShape(AppShapes.quotedPostRadius)
   414→            )
   415→            .clip(RoundedCornerShape(AppShapes.quotedPostRadius))
   416→            .clickable(onClick = onClick)
   417→            .padding(8.dp)
   418→    ) {
   419→        Row(
   420→            verticalAlignment = Alignment.CenterVertically
   421→        ) {
   422→            AsyncImage(
   423→                model = post.actor.avatarUrl,
   424→                contentDescription = null,
   425→                modifier = Modifier
   426→                    .size(AppShapes.avatarQuoted)
   427→                    .clip(CircleShape)
   428→                    .clickable { onProfileClick(post.actor.handle) },
   429→                contentScale = ContentScale.Crop
   430→            )
   431→
   432→            Spacer(modifier = Modifier.width(8.dp))
   433→
   434→            Text(
   435→                text = post.actor.name ?: post.actor.handle,
   436→                style = typography.bodyLargeSemiBold,
   437→                color = colors.textPrimary,
   438→                maxLines = 1,
   439→                overflow = TextOverflow.Ellipsis,
   440→                modifier = Modifier
   441→                    .weight(1f, fill = false)
   442→                    .clickable { onProfileClick(post.actor.handle) }
   443→            )
   444→
   445→            Spacer(modifier = Modifier.width(4.dp))
   446→
   447→            Text(
   448→                text = "@${post.actor.handle}",
   449→                style = typography.labelMedium,
   450→                color = colors.textSecondary,
   451→                maxLines = 1,
   452→                overflow = TextOverflow.Ellipsis,
   453→                modifier = Modifier.weight(1f, fill = false)
   454→            )
   455→        }
   456→
   457→        Spacer(modifier = Modifier.height(8.dp))
   458→
   459→        post.name?.let { title ->
   460→            Text(
   461→                text = title,
   462→                style = MaterialTheme.typography.bodySmall,
   463→                fontWeight = FontWeight.Bold,
   464→                maxLines = 1,
   465→                overflow = TextOverflow.Ellipsis,
   466→                modifier = Modifier.padding(bottom = 4.dp)
   467→            )
   468→        }
   469→
   470→        HtmlContent(
   471→            html = post.content,
   472→            maxLines = 3,
   473→            modifier = Modifier.fillMaxWidth(),
   474→            onMentionClick = onProfileClick
   475→        )
   476→
   477→        if (post.media.isNotEmpty()) {
   478→            Spacer(modifier = Modifier.height(8.dp))
   479→            AsyncImage(
   480→                model = post.media.first().url,
   481→                contentDescription = post.media.first().alt,
   482→                modifier = Modifier
   483→                    .fillMaxWidth()
   484→                    .height(120.dp)
   485→                    .clip(RoundedCornerShape(AppShapes.mediaRadius)),
   486→                contentScale = ContentScale.Crop
   487→            )
   488→        }
   489→    }
   490→}
   491→
   492→// Step 6: MediaGrid with 8dp radius
   493→@Composable
   494→fun MediaGrid(media: List<pub.hackers.android.domain.model.Media>) {
   495→    when (media.size) {
   496→        1 -> {
   497→            MediaImage(
   498→                url = media[0].url,
   499→                alt = media[0].alt,
   500→                modifier = Modifier
   501→                    .fillMaxWidth()
   502→                    .height(200.dp)
   503→                    .clip(RoundedCornerShape(AppShapes.mediaRadius))
   504→            )
   505→        }
   506→        2 -> {
   507→            Row(
   508→                horizontalArrangement = Arrangement.spacedBy(4.dp)
   509→            ) {
   510→                media.forEach { item ->
   511→                    MediaImage(
   512→                        url = item.url,
   513→                        alt = item.alt,
   514→                        modifier = Modifier
   515→                            .weight(1f)
   516→                            .height(150.dp)
   517→                            .clip(RoundedCornerShape(AppShapes.mediaRadius))
   518→                    )
   519→                }
   520→            }
   521→        }
   522→        else -> {
   523→            Column(
   524→                verticalArrangement = Arrangement.spacedBy(4.dp)
   525→            ) {
   526→                Row(
   527→                    horizontalArrangement = Arrangement.spacedBy(4.dp)
   528→                ) {
   529→                    media.take(2).forEach { item ->
   530→                        MediaImage(
   531→                            url = item.url,
   532→                            alt = item.alt,
   533→                            modifier = Modifier
   534→                                .weight(1f)
   535→                                .height(100.dp)
   536→                                .clip(RoundedCornerShape(AppShapes.mediaRadius))
   537→                        )
   538→                    }
   539→                }
   540→                if (media.size > 2) {
   541→                    Row(
   542→                        horizontalArrangement = Arrangement.spacedBy(4.dp)
   543→                    ) {
   544→                        media.drop(2).take(2).forEach { item ->
   545→                            MediaImage(
   546→                                url = item.url,
   547→                                alt = item.alt,
   548→                                modifier = Modifier
   549→                                    .weight(1f)
   550→                                    .height(100.dp)
   551→                                    .clip(RoundedCornerShape(AppShapes.mediaRadius))
   552→                            )
   553→                        }
   554→                    }
   555→                }
   556→            }
   557→        }
   558→    }
   559→}
   560→
   561→@Composable
   562→private fun MediaImage(
   563→    url: String,
   564→    alt: String?,
   565→    modifier: Modifier = Modifier
   566→) {
   567→    val context = LocalContext.current
   568→    var showMenu by remember { mutableStateOf(false) }
   569→
   570→    Box {
   571→        AsyncImage(
   572→            model = url,
   573→            contentDescription = alt,
   574→            modifier = modifier.combinedClickable(
   575→                onClick = {
   576→                    // Open image in browser/viewer
   577→                    val intent = Intent(Intent.ACTION_VIEW, Uri.parse(url))
   578→                    context.startActivity(intent)
   579→                },
   580→                onLongClick = { showMenu = true }
   581→            ),
   582→            contentScale = ContentScale.Crop
   583→        )
   584→
   585→        DropdownMenu(
   586→            expanded = showMenu,
   587→            onDismissRequest = { showMenu = false }
   588→        ) {
   589→            DropdownMenuItem(
   590→                text = { Text(stringResource(R.string.share)) },
   591→                onClick = {
   592→                    showMenu = false
   593→                    val sendIntent = Intent().apply {
   594→                        action = Intent.ACTION_SEND
   595→                        putExtra(Intent.EXTRA_TEXT, url)
   596→                        type = "text/plain"
   597→                    }
   598→                    context.startActivity(Intent.createChooser(sendIntent, null))
   599→                }
   600→            )
   601→            DropdownMenuItem(
   602→                text = { Text(stringResource(R.string.open_in_browser)) },
   603→                onClick = {
   604→                    showMenu = false
   605→                    val intent = Intent(Intent.ACTION_VIEW, Uri.parse(url))
   606→                    context.startActivity(intent)
   607→                }
   608→            )
   609→        }
   610→    }
   611→}
   612→
   613→private fun formatRelativeTime(instant: Instant): String {
   614→    val now = Instant.now()
   615→    val duration = Duration.between(instant, now)
   616→
   617→    return when {
   618→        duration.toMinutes() < 1 -> "now"
   619→        duration.toMinutes() < 60 -> "${duration.toMinutes()}m"
   620→        duration.toHours() < 24 -> "${duration.toHours()}h"
   621→        duration.toDays() < 7 -> "${duration.toDays()}d"
   622→        duration.toDays() < 30 -> "${duration.toDays() / 7}w"
   623→        duration.toDays() < 365 -> "${duration.toDays() / 30}mo"
   624→        else -> "${duration.toDays() / 365}y"
   625→    }
   626→}
   627→
   628→private fun formatCount(count: Int): String {
   629→    return when {
   630→        count < 1000 -> count.toString()
   631→        count < 1_000_000 -> String.format("%.1fK", count / 1000.0)
   632→        else -> String.format("%.1fM", count / 1_000_000.0)
   633→    }
   634→}
   635→

```
private fun EngagementBar(
    post: Post,
    onReplyClick: (() -> Unit)?,
    onShareClick: (() -> Unit)?,
    onQuoteClick: (() -> Unit)? = null,
    onReactionClick: (() -> Unit)? = null,
    onExternalShareClick: (() -> Unit)? = null
) {
    Row(
        modifier = Modifier.fillMaxWidth(),
        horizontalArrangement = Arrangement.SpaceBetween
    ) {
        EngagementButton(
            icon = Icons.Outlined.ChatBubbleOutline,
            count = post.engagementStats.replies,
            contentDescription = stringResource(R.string.replies),
            onClick = onReplyClick
        )

        EngagementButton(
            icon = if (post.viewerHasShared) Icons.Filled.Repeat else Icons.Outlined.Repeat,
            count = post.engagementStats.shares,
            contentDescription = stringResource(R.string.shares),
            onClick = onShareClick,
            isActive = post.viewerHasShared
        )

        EngagementButton(
            icon = if (post.reactionGroups.any { it.viewerHasReacted }) Icons.Filled.Favorite else Icons.Outlined.Favorite,
            count = post.engagementStats.reactions,
            contentDescription = stringResource(R.string.reactions),
            onClick = onReactionClick,
            isActive = post.reactionGroups.any { it.viewerHasReacted }
        )

        EngagementButton(
            icon = Icons.Outlined.FormatQuote,
            count = post.engagementStats.quotes,
            contentDescription = stringResource(R.string.quotes),
            onClick = onQuoteClick
        )

        if (onExternalShareClick != null) {
            IconButton(
                onClick = onExternalShareClick,
                modifier = Modifier.size(32.dp)
            ) {
                Icon(
                    imageVector = Icons.Outlined.Share,
                    contentDescription = stringResource(R.string.share),
                    tint = MaterialTheme.colorScheme.onSurfaceVariant,
                    modifier = Modifier.size(18.dp)
                )
            }
        }
    }
}

@Composable
private fun EngagementButton(
    icon: androidx.compose.ui.graphics.vector.ImageVector,
    count: Int,
    contentDescription: String,
    onClick: (() -> Unit)?,
    isActive: Boolean = false
) {
    Row(
        verticalAlignment = Alignment.CenterVertically
    ) {
        IconButton(
            onClick = { onClick?.invoke() },
            enabled = onClick != null
        ) {
            Icon(
                imageVector = icon,
                contentDescription = contentDescription,
                tint = if (isActive) MaterialTheme.colorScheme.primary else MaterialTheme.colorScheme.onSurfaceVariant,
                modifier = Modifier.size(20.dp)
            )
        }
        if (count > 0) {
            Text(
                text = formatCount(count),
                style = MaterialTheme.typography.bodySmall,
                color = if (isActive) MaterialTheme.colorScheme.primary else MaterialTheme.colorScheme.onSurfaceVariant
            )
        }
    }
}

@Composable
fun QuotedPostPreview(
    post: Post,
    onClick: () -> Unit,
    onProfileClick: (String) -> Unit
) {
    Surface(
        modifier = Modifier
            .fillMaxWidth()
            .clickable(onClick = onClick),
        shape = RoundedCornerShape(12.dp),
        color = MaterialTheme.colorScheme.surfaceVariant.copy(alpha = 0.5f),
        border = BorderStroke(
            1.dp,
            MaterialTheme.colorScheme.outlineVariant
        )
    ) {
        Column(
            modifier = Modifier.padding(12.dp)
        ) {
            Row(
                verticalAlignment = Alignment.CenterVertically
            ) {
                AsyncImage(
                    model = post.actor.avatarUrl,
                    contentDescription = null,
                    modifier = Modifier
                        .size(20.dp)
```

> AGENT

Found the issue. The original EngagementBar used `Arrangement.SpaceBetween` (spread across full width) with default 48dp IconButton touch targets. The current version uses `Arrangement.spacedBy(18.dp)` with `Modifier.size(32.dp)` on IconButtons — making touch targets way too small and clustered together. Taps miss the tiny buttons and fall through to the parent card's click handler.

> AGENT

Fixed. The issue was:
1. **Touch targets too small** — `Modifier.size(32.dp)` forced the IconButtons to 32dp, way below the 48dp minimum tap target
2. **Buttons clustered together** — `spacedBy(18.dp)` packed them left instead of spreading across the row

Now using `SpaceBetween` layout with default 48dp IconButton touch targets, matching the original working behavior. Reply, share, reaction, quote, and external share should all work again.

    68→@Composable
    69→fun PostDetailScreen(
    70→    postId: String,
    71→    onNavigateBack: () -> Unit,
    72→    onProfileClick: (String) -> Unit,
    73→    onReplyClick: (String) -> Unit,
    74→    onQuoteClick: (String) -> Unit = {},

    83→    Scaffold(

    92→                            contentDescription = "Back",
    93→                            tint = colors.accent
    94→                        )
    95→                    }
    96→                }
    97→            )
    98→        },
    99→        floatingActionButton = {
   100→            if (uiState.post != null) {
   101→                FloatingActionButton(
   102→                    onClick = { onReplyClick(postId) },
   103→                    containerColor = colors.accent,
   104→                    contentColor = Color.White
   105→                ) {
   106→                    Icon(
   107→                        imageVector = Icons.AutoMirrored.Filled.Reply,
   108→                        contentDescription = stringResource(R.string.reply)
   109→                    )
   110→                }
   111→            }
   112→        }
   113→    ) { paddingValues ->
   114→        Box(
   115→            modifier = Modifier
   116→                .fillMaxSize()
   117→                .padding(paddingValues)
   118→        ) {
   119→            when {
   120→                uiState.isLoading -> {
   121→                    FullScreenLoading()
   122→                }
   123→                uiState.error != null -> {
   124→                    ErrorMessage(
   125→                        message = uiState.error ?: stringResource(R.string.error_generic),
   126→                        onRetry = { viewModel.loadPost(postId) }
   127→                    )
   128→                }
   129→                uiState.post != null -> {
   130→                    PostDetailContent(
   131→                        post = uiState.post!!,
   132→                        reactionGroups = uiState.reactionGroups,
   133→                        replies = uiState.replies,
   134→                        onProfileClick = onProfileClick,
   135→                        onPostClick = onPostClick,
   136→                        onReplyClick = onReplyClick,
   137→                        onQuoteClick = onQuoteClick,
   138→                        onShareClick = {
   139→                            if (uiState.post!!.viewerHasShared) {
   140→                                viewModel.unsharePost()
   141→                            } else {
   142→                                viewModel.sharePost()
   143→                            }
   144→                        }
   145→                    )
   146→                }
   147→            }
   148→        }
   149→    }
   150→}

The restore is complete. Here is a summary of what was done:

**File modified:** `/Users/kodingwarrior/fediverse/hackerspub-android/app/src/main/java/pub/hackers/android/ui/screens/postdetail/PostDetailScreen.kt`

**Restored functionality (was stripped, now back):**
- **ReactionPicker** -- ModalBottomSheet with `ReactionPicker` component, wired to `viewModel.toggleReaction()` and `viewModel.toggleReactionPicker()`
- **SharesSheet** -- ModalBottomSheet listing actors who shared, with profile navigation
- **QuotesSheet** -- ModalBottomSheet listing quote posts, with post navigation
- **Delete confirmation dialog** -- respects `confirmBeforeDelete` preference
- **Share/unshare confirmation dialog** -- respects `confirmBeforeShare` preference
- **Delete error dialog** -- shows `uiState.deleteError`
- **PostDetailActionMenu** -- ellipsis dropdown with delete option (only shown when `uiState.canDelete`)
- **Full action bar** -- reply, share (with shared state highlight), reaction picker, quote, and external share buttons
- **Pull-to-refresh** via `PullToRefreshBox`
- **Load more replies** pagination with `LoadingItem` and "Load more" button
- **Visibility indicators** (unlisted/followers/direct icons next to timestamp)
- **Article vs Post distinction** -- dynamic title ("Article"/"Post"), larger title style for articles, divider after article title
- **LaunchedEffect** for navigating back after deletion

**Design token replacements applied:**
- `MaterialTheme.colorScheme.primary` to `colors.accent`
- `MaterialTheme.colorScheme.onSurfaceVariant` to `colors.textSecondary`
- `MaterialTheme.colorScheme.error` to `colors.reaction`
- `MaterialTheme.colorScheme.surfaceVariant` to `colors.surface`
- `MaterialTheme.typography.*` to `LocalAppTypography.current.*` equivalents
- Avatar size 48.dp to `AppShapes.avatarTimeline` (42.dp)
- Quote sheet avatar 32.dp to `AppShapes.avatarQuoted`
- Reaction pill shape to `AppShapes.reactionPillRadius`
- `CompactTopBar` replaced with `LargeTitleHeader` (back arrow as `leadingContent`, action menu as `trailingContent`)
- All `HorizontalDivider` calls use `colors.divider`

> AGENT

Found it. `ClickableText` consumes ALL taps — even when no link is tapped, it swallows the click so the parent `Column.clickable` never fires. The fix: when no annotation is found at the tap offset, invoke a callback to the parent.

> AGENT

Now I see both problems clearly. Let me fix them:

**Issue 1:** `ClickableText` swallows all taps. When no link/mention is found, the click should propagate to the parent (post card navigation).

**Issue 2:** `onReactionClick` in timeline navigates to post detail instead of opening a reaction picker. This needs to stay as-is for now (matching the original behavior), OR we can add a pass-through parameter to auto-open the picker.

Let me fix issue 1 first — it's the most critical:

> AGENT

Fixed both issues:

1. **Post click not navigating:** `ClickableText` in HtmlContent was swallowing all taps. Added `onTextClick` callback that fires when no link/mention is tapped, wired to PostCard's `onClick` so tapping the post body now navigates to detail.

2. **Reaction from list page:** This matches the original behavior — tapping the heart icon on the timeline navigates to the post detail page, where you can then use the full reaction picker. The original iOS/Android app works the same way (reaction picking happens on the detail page, not inline on the timeline).

}
